# UNIVERSAL IMPORTS — PLAN & LIVE STATUS
> **Nothing gets in without clearance.**
> The Agent Harness Hackathon · WeMakeDevs × TrueFoundry × Qodo · 24–30 Aug 2026
> **Deadline: 30 Aug 20:00 London = 31 Aug 00:30 IST. We submit 30 Aug, 18:00 IST.**

**This is the only planning file. Everything lives here: the plan, the task board, progress,
decisions, errors, and the traps. Claude Code reads and updates it every session.**

---

# ⚡ HOW THIS FILE WORKS

1. **§1 LIVE STATUS** is the top of every session. Claude reads it first and reports it back.
2. **§2 NEXT UP** always holds **exactly 4 tasks**. Claude presents them as a numbered menu and
   waits for you to pick one. Never more than one at a time.
3. When a task finishes: Claude moves it to **§4 DONE** with a result line, deletes it from
   NEXT UP, pulls replacements from **§11 BACKLOG**, and shows the new menu.
4. Anything learned goes into **§5 BLOCKED**, **§6 DECISIONS**, or **§7 ERRORS** as it happens —
   never batched for later.
5. If the plan turns out to be wrong, it goes in **§8 SUGGESTED CHANGES**, gets raised at
   standup, and someone edits the plan sections below. **The plan is allowed to change; drifting
   silently is not.**

Sections §1–§8 are living. Sections §9–§18 are the reference and change only by decision.

---

Previous entry: 2026-08-29 22:51 · **T-030 (PR #67) Qodo fix round — 4 real findings, all fixed (§4 for the full writeup).** The significant one: Mailpit's `PUT /api/v1/tags` *overwrites* rather than appends, so the original design would have silently erased any tag a message already had. Also fixed: a truncation-flag gap the redesign's own list-trimming could hide from the shared cap helper (and a second-order version of the same bug found while fixing it, not by Qodo); raw HTML from a non-200 response could reach the model unprocessed; an unbounded message-batch now caps at 100. 2026-08-26 · T-023 (Qodo pass) · [O1] · Qodo's PR #13 review found 1 real Bug: the initial `harness/README.md`/PLAN.md wording documented the three-subagent topology as if `dynamic_sub_agents` platform-enforces it. Independently verified against TrueForge's own docs (`trueforge.dev/key-features/subagents`) — confirmed the finding: the root agent's own model decides at runtime whether/how to delegate, generating its own instructions via the built-in `create_sub_agent` tool (not from our prose), and every spawned subagent gets the **full** root tool set — no per-agent scoping exists. Fixed by rewording README.md/this file to state plainly that `instructions` is best-effort prompt guidance to the root's delegation decision, not enforced configuration — "IDENTITY: no tool call" is a request, not a boundary; "exactly three subagents" is what we're asking for, not a guarantee. The approach itself (prompt-only, no custom delegation code) stays correct — Qodo's own assessment agreed building real enforcement would duplicate native harness behavior. `agent.json`'s `instructions` text itself wasn't changed — it was already phrased as guidance to the model, not a claim about platform behavior; only the docs describing it were overclaiming

2026-08-27 · T-024 (Qodo pass) · [O1] · Fixed 2 real bugs Qodo's PR #17 review found, both verified directly against `contracts/events.ts` and `cockpit/src/missionSource.ts`'s runtime validator before fixing, not just accepted on Qodo's say-so: (1) IDENTITY's field list omitted the required `from_address` and included a `return_path` the contract doesn't have — `IdentityEvidence` (and `isIdentityEvidence`'s runtime check) requires exactly `from_address`/`display_name`/`reply_to`/`lookalike_domain`/`lookalike_of`; a missing `from_address` genuinely fails Cockpit's validator (`fail(index, ...)`), confirmed by reading the check, not assumed. Fixed by adding `from_address` and keeping `return_path` as a bonus field alongside it (validator doesn't reject extras, only checks the required ones are present and typed). (2) The "never a field another subagent owns" line was genuinely ambiguous: `domain_intel` and `correspondence_history` both have their own `domain` field, and `CorrespondenceHistory`'s contract requires HISTORY's `domain` — a literal reading of "ownership" could tell the model to drop HISTORY's required `domain` just because a different tool's output happens to share that key name. Reworded to make "own remit" explicitly about judgment (only IDENTITY decides lookalike-domain, only INFRASTRUCTURE calls its three tools), not field names — a shared key name across two tools' own outputs is never a reason to drop or rename either one. `harness/README.md` updated to match both fixes. `agent.json` re-validated as JSON; `node --test` re-run clean (13/13, unaffected). Pushed to PR #17, not merged — waiting on Qodo to re-review the new HEAD

Fixed 6, deferred 3 with recorded reasoning, all within `tools/` only (no `contracts/`/`cockpit/`/`harness/` touched). **Fixed:** (1) `parse_message`'s real output didn't match `ParsedMessage`/Cockpit's runtime validator at all — `from`/`reply_to`/`return_path` were nested `{display_name, address}` objects instead of scalar strings, `authentication_results` was a list instead of a string, and `message_id`/top-level `display_name` were missing entirely; unlike `DomainIntel`/`UrlReputation` (below), nothing merged elsewhere depends on this shape yet, so fixed the producer (`normaliser.py`) to match the existing contract instead of deferring — `from`/`reply_to`/`return_path` now plain address strings, `display_name` taken from the From header only, `authentication_results` is multiple headers joined with `\n`, `message_id` from the `Message-ID` header. `server.py`'s `_cap_response` simplified to match (the old dict-shaped address-trimming branch is dead code now — removed, not left unreachable). (2) `_extract_urls` promised "every URL from every body alternative" but `get_body(preferencelist=...)` only returns one best-match part per content type, silently skipping a secondary/nested multipart/alternative group (e.g. a forwarded message embedded as its own part) — rewritten to `msg.walk()` every leaf part, still collecting HTML before plain-text so the existing "HTML anchor wins the dedup" preference holds regardless of document order. (3+8) `url_reputation`'s `_cap_response` only ever trimmed `tags`/`note`/`url`, leaving `threat`/`url_status`/`date_added` unbounded — a oversized untrimmed field could keep the response over the 2KB cap forever, and worse, `note`/`url`'s own shrink step (`max(1, len(value)-200)`) plateaued at one leftover character instead of reaching `""`, so the loop never terminated once a field got that short while the response was still oversized. Rewritten with a `_shrink_string` that strictly shrinks toward `""` (mirrors `server.py`'s own pattern) applied to all five upstream scalar fields, with a final fits-check after each. (4) `domain_intel`'s `_cap_response_size` measured the payload *before* adding `truncated: false`, so a result exactly at the boundary could return a few bytes over the declared 2KB cap while still claiming untruncated — now builds the full flagged payload first, checks that, and re-verifies after truncating (with a second, more aggressive truncation pass if the first round isn't enough). (6) `url_reputation`'s listed-result path copied URLhaus's `tags` field without checking it was a list of strings — a malformed-but-valid-JSON response (e.g. `tags: 123`) would crash `_cap_response`'s `list(tags)` call outright; now validated and degraded to `available: false` before ever reaching a caller, same "valid JSON, wrong shape" guard `domain_intel` already applies to RDAP's `events`. (7) `test_server_integration.py`'s live `domain_intel` RDAP/crt.sh call had no gate (unlike the URLhaus test, which already skips without a key) despite PLAN.md's own §7 recording that this exact call reproducibly times out a later test in the same shared server subprocess — gated behind `RUN_LIVE_DOMAIN_INTEL_TESTS=1`, off by default, same pattern as the URLhaus gate. (10) `domain_intel`'s server wrapper only checked for empty/whitespace before interpolating the caller's string into the RDAP URL path — a domain containing `/`, `?`, `#` would query a different path/query than intended; added an RFC-1035-shaped hostname regex + 253-char length check at the tool boundary, before `_domain_intel` ever runs. 25 new regression tests across `test_normaliser.py`/`test_domain_intel.py`/`test_url_reputation.py`/`test_server_domain_intel_contract.py`/`test_server_contract.py` (existing `_cap_response`/contract tests in `test_server_contract.py` also updated for the new flat shape) — 101/101 pass, 1 correctly skipped (the newly-gated live test; confirmed it still passes when explicitly opted in). `harness` 13/13, `contracts`/`cockpit` unaffected (untouched). **Deferred, not fixed here (finding #2):** `domain_intel`'s real nested `rdap`/`cert` output still doesn't match `contracts/events.ts`'s flat `DomainIntel` — Qodo independently rediscovered exactly the drift already logged in §5/§8 (2026-08-27). Left as-is on purpose: `harness/agent.json`'s merged T-023/T-024 instructions already deliberately target `domain_intel`'s *current* real field names verbatim (§4, T-024 entry) — flattening the tool now would silently break that already-reviewed prompt guidance, and the actual fix (`contracts/events.ts`) needs 2 approvals and is out of this PR's scope regardless. Qodo's own high-level assessment on this PR agreed: "that focused follow-up should land before live domain-intelligence events reach Cockpit" — separate `/contracts` PR, not this one. **Declined (findings #9, #11):** `.gitignore`'s tools-artifact entries weren't named in the original PR description — fixed by updating the PR description directly (cheap, no code change needed). The 400-line-PR rule violation (finding #11) stands as previously documented (§6, 2026-08-27 integration entry) — an integration merge carrying already-individually-Qodo-reviewed commit history isn't the same class of risk as one large unreviewed diff; no code change, position restated on the PR

2026-08-29 · T-026 · [O2] · **`detonate(url)` wired as the fourth read-only `imports-mcp` tool — Python port of `harness/detonate.js` (T-014), not a new design.** Resolved the apparent T-026↔T-035 ordering concern (§5/§8) before starting: that's about the *future* Daytona-sandbox execution path, not this task — `detonate.js` is confirmed text-mode only (no Daytona), and §10's own MCP tool table already places `detonate(url)` in this Python surface. `tools/imports_mcp/detonate.py` follows redirects manually (max 10 hops), refuses non-http(s) schemes and private/loopback/link-local targets (SSRF guard, Rule 2880752 — via stdlib `ipaddress.is_private` against the *resolved* address, catching DNS-rebinding domains too, not just literal `127.0.0.1`-style URLs), streams the body under a 2MB cap before parsing, and flags `<form>`s that ask for a password and post cross-domain, via `lxml` (never regex, trap #5). Every failure mode returns `{url, redirect_chain, error}` instead of raising, matching every other tool's degrade-not-raise convention; response capped under ~2KB (Rule 2880706) with the same strictly-shrinking-toward-`""` pattern `url_reputation`'s fix established (PR #19), reused here from the start rather than found broken later. Two real Python-specific bugs found and fixed against detonate.js's own port target, not assumed: (1) `urljoin`/`urlparse` raise `ValueError` for a malformed bracketed-authority string (`http://[`) instead of returning an inert result the way JS's `new URL()` effectively degrades to via the existing try/catch — now caught per-form (falls into the existing `action_invalid` path) and around the redirect-Location join. (2) `requests` builds `Response.next` internally *even with* `allow_redirects=False`, which eagerly parses a malformed `Location` header itself and raises a bare `ValueError` straight out of `requests.get()` — not a `RequestException` — before this module ever inspects the header itself; caught alongside `RequestException`. One resulting behavioral difference from the JS original, verified not assumed: on that specific malformed-redirect path, `redirect_chain` ends up empty here (the exception fires before the hop can be recorded) where the JS version records one hop first — both still return a structured error, never raise, which is the actual contract. 22 new tests total, verified together (166 passed, 2 skipped) but **split across two PRs to stay under the ~400-line guideline** — same reason T-020's PR #9/#14 split, and per §7's own logged lesson from that split ("write the §4 entry to say explicitly which PR each part landed in"), stated explicitly here rather than left implicit: **this PR** (`tools/t026-detonate`, implementation) carries `tools/imports_mcp/detonate.py`, the `server.py` wiring, `test_server_detonate_contract.py` (tool-wrapper boundary), and 2 always-on transport tests in `test_server_integration.py` (including an SSRF-guard check needing no live network call or opt-in) — 459 lines. **A follow-up PR**, branched from this one, carries only `test_detonate.py` — the exhaustive local-fixture-server suite mirroring `detonate.test.js`'s scenarios one-to-one (§13 safety rules: never a real domain) — 243 lines. Both required for T-026 to actually be complete; the 166/2 count above only holds once both are merged. `harness` 18/18, unaffected (no harness/ file touched — `detonate.js` itself is untouched, this is a same-behavior port, not a replacement). Picked and finished inside its 2h timebox (§3, started 2026-08-29 00:01, done 00:11). §2 refilled with **T-032** — did not pull T-030, which is described as "`quarantine` over IMAP" and likely carries the exact same Mailpit-has-no-IMAP staleness T-022 already hit (§5); flagged, not silently picked
2026-08-29 · T-026 (Qodo pass on PR #37) · [O2] · Qodo's review found 4 Bugs + 2 Rule violations. Fixed 3, deferred 1 with reasoning, declined 2 citing existing precedent — all within `tools/`, no cross-folder edit. **Fixed:** (1) *Malformed starting URLs still raised* — the two `ValueError`-guards already added (redirect Location, form action) missed the very first `urlparse(current_url)`/`.hostname` access at the top of the loop; a malformed bracketed-authority *starting* URL (e.g. `http://[`) raised straight out of `detonate()`, the one path not already exercised by a test. Now wrapped the same way, degrading to `{url, redirect_chain, error: "malformed URL: ..."}`. (2) *Password casing bypassed detection* — `input[@type="password"]` was a case-sensitive XPath equality check, but HTML's `type` is an enumerated attribute matched case-insensitively per spec; `type="PASSWORD"` is a real password field in every browser and was silently missed. Replaced with a case-insensitive scan over every `<input>` in the form. (3) *Equivalent origins flagged cross-domain* — origins were compared as raw `scheme://netloc` strings, so `https://example.com` and `https://example.com:443` (the same origin — browsers normalize this away in `URL.origin`) were falsely flagged cross-domain; could mask or invent suspicious-form signal. New `_normalize_origin()` strips the default port per scheme and excludes any userinfo, used for both `action_origin` and `page_origin` (the `_origin()` helper now delegates to it) — same helper, one place to get right. Also tempers this entry's own earlier claim: the SSRF guard's "catches DNS-rebinding domains too" line (above) is *narrower* than it reads — see the deferred item below. 3 new regression tests, added to `test_server_detonate_contract.py` rather than `test_detonate.py` (that file lives on the still-open stacked PR #38 — a same-named file here would collide with it; flagged for whoever merges both to consider consolidating). Full suite: **156 passed, 2 skipped** on this branch (`test_detonate.py`'s 22 aren't part of it); `harness` 18/18, unaffected. **Deferred (finding #3, DNS rebinding/TOCTOU — real, not fixed here):** `_is_private_target()` resolves and validates one DNS answer, but `requests.get()` re-resolves the same hostname independently when connecting — a malicious authoritative DNS server could answer public for the check and private for the connection, defeating the guard. Decision + rationale in §6. **Declined, citing existing precedent (not code-changed):** finding #5 (`allow_private_network_targets` "violates Rule 2880752") and finding #6 (PR still 459 lines after one split) — both replied to on the PR citing §8's existing entries for the same rule/finding class; see §8 for the citations

A 4th finding, found on PR #34's own review, was real too: `approval_resolved`'s optional `reason` (validated contract data) was captured but never surfaced, reducing a denial to generic "DENIED" text — fixed, now appended to the gate's detail whenever present. **One Qodo finding was stale, not fixed:** PR #32's final review still cited the pre-rebase PR size (464 lines) for the size-rule finding specifically, even though every other finding in the same pass correctly re-scanned the rebased HEAD — corrected in a PR comment citing GitHub's own `additions`/`deletions` count (119+81=200) rather than waiting on an in-thread dismiss mechanism that had no thread to attach to this time (§7 has the general lesson). `npm run build` clean on every pushed commit; full `buildMissionPlan` logic re-verified against the real fixture after each fix (all checkpoints, denial path, missing-approval_required path, empty-summary path, reason-present path) via throwaway `npx tsx` scripts, none committed. 2026-08-28 · T-053 · [O3] · **Detonation panel — redirect chain, forms, summary, merged into `main` as `94f90ea60f2418acddd9c75aaf1e0e3bd17e7104` (PR #41).** New `cockpit/src/DetonationPanel.tsx` renders the full `DetonationResult`: hop-by-hop redirect chain with the final URL highlighted, every form's action/method/cross-domain/asks-password fields, and the summary — the exact "asks for a password and posts it to a different domain" combination (§17, 0:45-1:25) is called out with its own warning style, not just plain text. Both `DetonationResult` branches handled (error, success) and `DetonationForm`'s `action_invalid` case. **The screenshot half is deliberately not faked:** `screenshot_id` is a documented stretch goal `detonate.js` never sets (contracts/events.ts's own comment), and no image-serving mechanism exists anywhere in this repo — no endpoint, no static-path convention, nothing decided in PLAN.md. Rather than guess at an API that doesn't exist (CLAUDE.md), renders an honest "No screenshot — text-mode detonation (T-001/T-035, §5)" state when absent (currently always, including in the fixture), and just the identifier — not a fabricated `<img src>` — if `screenshot_id` is ever present. **Qodo caught 1 real Bug:** both `DetonationPanel.tsx` and `missionPlan.ts`'s own `detonationEvent` lookup used `events.find()`, which returns the *first* matching event — so a retried/re-emitted detonation would leave the panel and tree permanently stuck on the stale first result, never showing anything more current. Inconsistent with how gate/lane state already track the stream's current state everywhere else in this app. Fixed in both places with `findLast()`. **A related, same-class bug was found but deliberately left unfixed, out of this task's scope:** `missionPlan.ts`'s `messageEvent`/`verdictEvent` lookups use the identical `events.find()` pattern on different event types — not touched here since T-053 only concerns detonation, logged below for whoever picks it up. `npm run build` clean on both pushed commits. Verified: `react-dom/server`'s `renderToStaticMarkup` checked the pending state, the real fixture's success case (every field confirmed present in the markup, including the dangerous-form warning and the honest no-screenshot state), a synthetic error branch, the `action_invalid` form case, an empty-forms case, a `screenshot_id`-present case confirming no `<img src>` gets guessed, and — after the fix — a synthetic two-event stream (a failed first attempt then a successful retry) confirming both the panel and the tree now show the *latest* result with the stale one gone from the markup entirely, plus the normal single-detonation case confirmed unaffected. Dev server smoke-tested. In-browser visual rendering still not independently verified — no browser tool available this session, same caveat as T-051/T-052

2026-08-29 · T-056 · [O3] · **Resumable-stream reconnect handling, merged into `main` as `4c83f5caa919711608209a38efccad3c9201609c` (PR #47).** New `cockpit/src/resumableStream.ts` implements and tests the confirmed reconnect mechanic (T-002/§6): `GET /turns/{id}/subscribe?after_sequence_number=N` resumes a dropped stream from the last sequence number seen, not from the start. Transport-agnostic — takes a pluggable "open a stream starting at sequence N" function, so it's genuinely testable without a live TrueForge connection. **Deliberately scoped to the reconnect mechanic alone, not the full live `trueForgeEventSource`:** building that also needs a translation layer from TrueForge's raw turn-stream events into this app's `mission.*` events, and nothing in `contracts/`or PLAN.md has ever defined that mapping (T-002 only confirmed generic session/turn/tool-approval mechanics) — inventing that mapping would be the same undocumented-API-guessing trap T-055 was blocked on, so it's logged in §8 below, not solved here. Qodo reviewed clean (0/0/0), no findings. `npm run build` clean. Verified via a throwaway `npx tsx` script (not committed), 5 scenarios: happy path (no drop); a mid-stream drop resuming from the correct offset with no lost/duplicated events; a resume-boundary duplicate (server re-sends its last item) correctly deduplicated; exceeding `maxReconnectAttempts` throws the real error with status `error`; exponential backoff delay genuinely measured growing between attempts. **Cross-owner discovery while selecting the next task:** T-036 (LICENCE REQUIRED panel — renders TrueForge's approval request in our styling, Allow/Deny per gate) has been sitting unpicked in §11's Slice 3 list this entire time, outside the "from join day" Cockpit list every previous O3 session scanned. **Only its presentation half is unblocked** — `contracts/fixtures/mission-happy-path.json` already carries real `mission.approval_required`/`mission.approval_resolved` events for all 4 gates, so the request panel + Allow/Deny UI is genuinely buildable now; the live-submission half needs the same undecided TrueForge→`mission.*` translation layer just logged above, so it's blocked, not done, and T-036's own §2 row says so explicitly (self-correction after Qodo caught this PR's first draft overclaiming "fully unblocked" — inconsistent with this very entry). Squarely in §17's demo script (1:50-2:20) for the presentation half. Picked next, ahead of T-043/T-044 which come later in the backlog order

2026-08-29 · T-033 · [O1, taking over from §11's O2 listing] · **`file_abuse_report` — the second of the four T-034 gate-target tools to exist.** Same disclosed cross-owner takeover as T-031: O2 was occupied with T-026 (PRs #37/#38) and T-022 (itself blocked, §5), and T-033 was verified unstarted — no branch, no PR, and `git grep "def file_abuse_report"` across *every* remote ref returned nothing. Signature `file_abuse_report(domain, evidence)` comes from §10's tool table and `contracts/fixtures/mission-happy-path.json` verbatim, so **`contracts/` needed no change** and its 2-approval rule was never engaged. Takes a *domain*, not an address: the abuse mailbox is resolved from RDAP via the merged `domain_intel` (T-020), whose flat top-level `abuse_contact` mirror (added in PR #19) this consumes. **Shared SMTP machinery extracted, not copied** — new `tools/imports_mcp/_smtp.py` now holds target resolution, address validation and response capping; `notify_impersonated` delegates to it with its public surface unchanged, and T-031's 43 tests passing untouched is the regression proof (run immediately after extraction, before any T-033 code). Extraction rather than duplication was deliberate: the port-0 guard (smtplib maps port 0 to **25**, the real outbound MX port) came from Qodo's PR #29 review, and two copies of a safety guard drift. **Safety is most of this task**, because unlike T-031's fictional `.example` recipient this tool mails *a real registrar's real abuse mailbox* straight from RDAP — exactly CLAUDE.md trap #6. So on top of the Range-by-default target it **refuses outright** unless the resolved SMTP host is loopback, unless `ALLOW_EXTERNAL_SMTP=1` is deliberately set (documented in `.env.example`; anything but exactly `"1"` is off); refusal is a *result*, not an exception, so the mission still learns why. RDAP is third-party data, so the returned contact is validated as strictly as a model-supplied one before becoming an envelope recipient. Missing contact degrades gracefully (§12/§13: "not published" is a finding, not a crash). Approval is never checked in-module — the harness gates it. **Qodo found 3 real bugs on first review, all mine, all fixed:** (1) a non-string RDAP `abuse_contact` (list/dict/int) raised `TypeError` out of `re.match`/`len`, breaking the "never raises" contract — confirmed by reproducing all three types; (2) a **DNS-rebinding window** — the guard resolved the host then handed the *hostname* to `smtplib`, which resolves it again, so a hostile resolver could swap in a routable address the guard never approved; now resolves once and connects to the validated loopback **literal**, and every `getaddrinfo` answer must be loopback; (3) the opt-in live test called **production `rdap.org`** and could never have shown a delivery anyway (a reserved `.example` domain publishes no abuse contact, so its own assertion was `sent: False`) — removed, with the root cause also fixed by moving the Range guard *before* the RDAP lookup so a refused send queries no third-party registry at all. **46 new tests** (30 unit + 15 contract + regressions); full `tools/` suite **198 passed / 3 skipped** from merged `main`, `harness` 18/18 unaffected — result: **merged into `main` as `71107ad33b22f793f022504dd0e91dab8fbb2696` (PR #40)** with Qodo clean of Bugs on the exact final HEAD `32c45c6` (0 Bugs / 0 Skill insights; the single remaining Rule violation is the deliberately accepted PR-size exception, documented in the PR on the PR #4/#19 precedent, because splitting would separate a mail-sending tool from the tests that are its only safety evidence). **Verification stated precisely: a REAL `smtplib` send over a REAL loopback TCP socket delivered successfully (evidence reference observed on the wire), which proves the SMTP path works unmocked — it does NOT prove a real abuse report was delivered to a registrar, and none was. No real registrar or abuse mailbox was contacted at any point.** The Mailpit live test could not be run: the Docker daemon was not running on this machine. Two of the four gated tools now exist; T-030/T-032 remain O2's and unstarted

2026-08-29 · T-040 (investigated, resolved without code) · [O1] · **Session persistence and resume are already provided by TrueForge — verified live, so there is nothing for us to build, and building it would have violated CLAUDE.md's own "don't rebuild what the harness already does" list (line 148 names *session persistence* and *reconnect survival* explicitly).** §5's T-055 row correctly flagged that nobody had ever actually checked TrueForge's live schema for a session-listing/history endpoint; this task checked. Against a real instance (WSL2), confirmed by response, not by reading docs: `GET /api/v1/sessions` returns **HTTP 200** with a real paginated list (`{"data":[],"pagination":{"limit":25}}`) and its summary in the live OpenAPI is literally "List sessions"; `GET /api/v1/sessions/{id}` and `GET /api/v1/sessions/{id}/events` are both real endpoints (proper `404 "Session not found"` for a bogus id, not a route miss); `GET /api/v1/sessions/{id}/turns`, `.../turns/{turn_id}`, `.../turns/{turn_id}/events` and `.../turns/{turn_id}/subscribe` all exist. **Persistence is on disk, not in memory:** the standalone instance keeps `~/.local/share/trueforge/db/db.sqlite`, and the file survived a full process restart with the server rebooting and serving `GET /sessions` again — so a campaign's sessions genuinely do survive a restart already. This **corrects an earlier O1 assessment** that called T-040 an invalid premise on the grounds that no such API had been confirmed: the API exists; what was wrong was the task's framing, not the goal. **The other half is still undefined and was deliberately not invented:** `dossier` and `campaign` appear **nowhere** in TrueForge's live spec (checked), §10's tool table has no dossier tool, no storage format or location is decided anywhere in this file, and §17's 3-minute demo script has no dossier beat at all. So "campaign dossier" remains an O1/O3 scope question (§8), not something to guess at — result: **no code written, none needed for the persistence half**; T-040's remaining scope is recorded in §8 and the task stays in §11 rather than being deleted or falsely marked complete. **Directly unblocks O3's T-055** (§5): its blocker's reason #2 — "no session-listing/history endpoint has ever been confirmed to exist on TrueForge's live schema; nothing in this repo has checked for one" — is now disproven with live evidence, and the Cockpit dossier view can bind to these endpoints. T-055's other stated reasons (no decided storage format, no §17 demo beat) are untouched and remain O3's call

2026-08-29 · T-043 · [O3] · **Spoken verdict — §17's closing beat (2:40-3:00, "A phone. Plain English, spoken.").** New `cockpit/src/SpokenVerdict.tsx` reads `mission.complete`'s `spoken_verdict` field (already the exact text this task needs — `contracts/events.ts`'s own field comment names T-043) and speaks it via the Web Speech API (`window.speechSynthesis`) on a manual "Speak it" button, not autoplay — firing speech the instant the event arrives would talk over a live presenter at that exact second, and unprompted audio is unreliable across browsers' autoplay policies anyway; a user gesture is the reliable path either way. Feature-detects `speechSynthesis` and renders an honest "isn't available" state when absent (same pattern as `DetonationPanel`'s screenshot branch) rather than crashing or guessing — this also means the component degrades correctly in a non-browser render (Node has no `window` at all), not just a browser that lacks the API. `findLast`, not `find`, for the same "current, not first" reason every other panel's event lookup already uses (§7). Wired into `App.tsx` as the last panel, after `MissionView`, matching its place as the demo's actual final beat. `npm run build` (`tsc -b` strict + `vite build`) clean. **Verified, not assumed:** a throwaway `npx tsx --tsconfig tsconfig.app.json` script (not committed) exercised `react-dom/server`'s `renderToStaticMarkup` across 3 scenarios — before `mission.complete` (pending state, no button); the full fixture (verbatim `spoken_verdict` text rendered, honest not-available state confirmed in this Node environment, no button shown); and a synthetic second `mission.complete` confirming `findLast` picks the later text over the first. Dev server smoke-tested — new module serves `200`. **No browser tool was available this session** (offered, declined by the user mid-task) — so the actual click→speak behavior, which only a real browser's Web Speech implementation can exercise, is unverified beyond the static-render/feature-detection checks above; flagging this explicitly rather than claiming a check that wasn't done. Not yet merged — this branch (`cockpit/t043-spoken-verdict`) is stacked on the still-unmerged T-036 branch (PR #52), pushed as its own PR against that branch; will need a rebase onto `main` once PR #52 lands

2026-08-29 · T-036 + T-043 (Qodo fix rounds, both merged) · [O3] · **Both PRs reviewed by Qodo, fixed, and merged into `main` — the two rows directly above are now stale on "not yet merged," left as the historical record of the implementation itself; this entry is the completion.** **PR #52 (T-036)** — Qodo's first pass found 3 real Bugs + 1 Rule violation, all now resolved: (1) the `LICENCE REQUIRED` banner used plain `orange` text, the identical ~1.97:1-contrast failure already fixed once for the verdict label (PR #43) — fixed to `#b45309`. (2) `buildApprovalGates()` only overwrote `action`/`request` on a fresh `approval_required` for a gate index that already carried a prior outcome, so a **retried** tool call (same class as T-053's retried-detonation lesson, §7) rendered as permanently stuck on the earlier attempt's `denied`/`executed` state instead of returning to "requested" with live controls — fixed by clearing `resolved`/`reason`/`resultSummary`/`executed` whenever a new request arrives for that index. (3) `role="status"` sat on the whole `<li>`, so a screen reader got the entire raw `tool_calls` JSON read aloud as a live announcement from four simultaneous regions at once — fixed by moving the live region to a small `sr-only` span carrying a short summary ("Gate 1 (Quarantine message): licence required"), leaving the visible card and its JSON outside any live region. (4) A Rule violation ("Approval actions are disabled") **dismissed, not code-fixed** — replied on the finding's own thread (same `@qodo dismiss`-with-reasoning mechanism as the PR #8/#4 precedents, §6) citing that this is PLAN.md's own documented scope split (§2/§8): the four sequential gates themselves are correctly implemented one-per-tool, only the live-submission wiring is disabled, and re-enabling it with nowhere real to submit to would be exactly the "guess at undocumented behavior" CLAUDE.md bars. All 4 findings independently re-verified `✓ Resolved`/`✗ Dismissed` by Qodo against the fixed HEAD (`a777d3a`) before merging — confirmed via the GitHub API, not assumed from the dashboard summary alone. **PR #53 (T-043)** — Qodo found 1 real Bug: `speaking` was only ever cleared by an utterance's own `onend`/`onerror` callback, but a documented WebKit regression means `cancel()` doesn't always fire either one — so if a newer `mission.complete` replaced the verdict while a stale utterance was still "cancelling," the Speak button could be stranded on "Speaking…" forever. Fixed by resetting `speaking` synchronously in the cleanup effect itself (not just calling `cancel()` and hoping for a callback), plus a ref-tracked "active utterance" so a late callback from a superseded attempt can no longer touch newer state. Re-verified clean (`✓ Resolved`) against the exact final HEAD (`dd33ecf`). **Merge sequence, verified from GitHub after each step, not assumed:** `main` was at `7dbb9eb` beforehand; PR #52 merged as `96b5e5076d65e62fcc4fcca4b5408cc71f761e10` (confirmed `state: MERGED`, `mergedAt` present, `origin/main` fast-forwarded to it); PR #53 — originally opened against the T-036 branch since it was stacked — was retargeted to `main` (`gh pr edit 53 --base main`) once #52 landed, re-confirmed `CLEAN`/`MERGEABLE` with its diff now scoped to only its own 4 files (`App.tsx`, `SpokenVerdict.tsx`, `index.css`, `PLAN.md`), rebuilt clean, then merged as `bcf951924d61ecadff3964be53482bef1f7e6c3a`. `main` now at `bcf9519`. Both feature branches left on the remote (not deleted) per the merge command used. §2/§3/§11 were already correctly updated during the two tasks' own sessions (T-036→T-043→T-044 swaps, §2's note stack) — untouched by this entry, only §4/§1 needed the actual merge confirmation

2026-08-29 · T-041 · [O3, disclosed cross-owner takeover from §11's O1 listing] · **Error paths — sandbox dies, MCP times out, model returns garbage, redirect loop — four named failure modes, each investigated on its own merits rather than assumed to need code.** O3's own backlog was genuinely empty (T-055 declined with user sign-off, T-044's remainder needs a browser tool this session doesn't have) — verified T-041 unstarted before picking it up (no branch, no PR, no commit touching it anywhere in the remote), same disclosed-takeover pattern O1 used for T-031/T-033. **Redirect loop:** `detonate.js`'s existing `MAX_REDIRECT_HOPS` cap already terminates a genuine A↔B loop, but had no direct regression test proving it (the existing tests only cover long chains of *distinct* URLs, never an actual loop) — added one (`detonate.test.js`), confirms the chain stops at exactly 10 recorded hops with the documented error, not a hang or an unbounded chain. **MCP times out:** already comprehensively handled at the tool-implementation level, verified by reading every module in `tools/imports_mcp` — RDAP, crt.sh, URLhaus, and both SMTP sends (`notify_impersonated`, `file_abuse_report`) each carry their own explicit timeout, and `detonate.js` has its own (`AbortSignal.timeout`). No gap found, no code needed — this sub-item was already done, just not previously credited to T-041. **Sandbox dies:** deliberately not built — CLAUDE.md's own "don't rebuild what the harness already does" list names sandbox lifecycle explicitly ("the agent requests a sandbox; the harness spins it up and tears it down"), the same class of mistake T-025 was caught making. **Model returns garbage:** the one genuine prompt gap — every tool already degrades to a structured `error`/`available: false` shape instead of raising, but `agent.json`'s `instructions` never told the model what to do with that field once it arrived. 2026-08-29 · T-026, PR #37 (Qodo re-review, round 3) · [O2] · **First synced this branch against current `main` (17 commits behind — T-033/T-036/T-040/T-041/T-043/T-044/T-055-declined all landed since the last sync), then triggered and fixed Qodo's re-review of the DNS-pinning fix itself: 5 new findings, 3 real bugs fixed with regression tests, 2 (already-litigated) rule findings re-declined citing the same standing precedent, not re-argued from scratch.** **Sync:** `git merge origin/main` — one real conflict, in `PLAN.md` (`.env.example`'s `ALLOW_EXTERNAL_SMTP` addition and `tools/imports_mcp/server.py`'s `notify_impersonated` wiring both auto-merged clean). Resolved the same way as the prior sync (§7, this pattern is now established): §1's `TODAY'S GOAL`/`BLOCKED ON` took `main`'s more current text; `LAST UPDATED`'s two independently-grown chains were interleaved by timestamp (this branch's 2 unique entries slotted between `main`'s 12:10 and 01:23 entries, where they chronologically belong — verified against every timestamp, not just concatenated); §2's NEXT UP table took `main`'s version outright, since it already correctly carried this exact PR's in-flight warning row (main's own cross-owner sync, 2026-08-29 17:23, had already found and flagged PR #37/#38); §6/§8 keep both sides' entries (different subjects, nothing conflicting in substance); §11's Slice 3 backlog list took `main`'s version (already reflects T-033/T-036 completed and removed). 19 section headers present exactly once, no conflict markers, `tools/` 214 passed/2 skipped and `harness` 19/19 both green *before* touching any fix. **Triggered the re-review manually**: 3 pushed commits (Qodo-fix, merge-sync, DNS-pinning-fix) produced no new Qodo activity at all — not stale evidence this time (§7, 2026-08-26/O1's finding), literally zero new comments or check-runs — so posted a `/review` PR comment (Qodo's own manual-trigger command), which did produce a fresh pass pinned to the current head. **Findings and outcomes:** (1) **"IDNs bypass DNS pinning" (High, fixed).** The pin key was the raw Unicode hostname from `urlparse`, but Requests IDNA-encodes a non-ASCII host (`PreparedRequest.prepare_url`) before the real connection — so an internationalized target's pin key and the hostname actually resolved at connect time diverged, falling through `_pinning_getaddrinfo` to a second, unpinned lookup. Fixed with `_idna_hostname()`, canonicalizing to `.encode('idna')` — the exact transform Requests itself applies — before both `_resolve_pinned_address` and `_pin_dns_resolution` see the hostname; a no-op for ordinary ASCII hosts. (2) **"Invalid form ports raise" (High, fixed).** `_normalize_origin()` reads `parsed.port`, which raises `ValueError` for an out-of-range/non-numeric port (`https://example.com:99999/`) — `urlparse` doesn't validate the port eagerly, so this only surfaced *after* the existing malformed-action guard had already exited normally, letting one hostile form's bad port escape `detonate()`'s documented never-raise contract. Fixed by wrapping the `_normalize_origin()` call in its own `try/except ValueError` inside `_extract_forms()`, folding into the same `action_invalid: true` path a malformed authority already takes — one bad port doesn't abort analysis of the page's other forms. (3) **"Proxies bypass DNS pinning" (High, fixed).** `requests.get()` honors `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` environment variables by default — routed through a proxy, the *proxy* performs its own independent DNS resolution of the target, so the local pin (which only intercepts `socket.getaddrinfo` for the pinned hostname) never even sees that lookup, reopening the exact DNS-rebinding gap pinning was built to close. Fixed with a new `_get_without_proxy_trust()` helper — a disposable `requests.Session()` with `trust_env = False`, otherwise byte-for-byte what `requests.get()` already does internally (open a session, make one call, close it), so no other behavior (streaming past the session close, timeouts, redirects) changed. (4) **"Local detonation bypass exposed" (Medium, declined, same precedent as before).** Same underlying code as finding #5 from the *first* review round (§8, 2026-08-29) — Qodo's own relevance note here says explicitly "no close historical acceptance or rejection precedent covers test-only private-target overrides," meaning its own similarity search didn't surface the row that already exists two sections up; declined again, same reasoning, full update in §8. (5) **"PR exceeds 400-line limit" (Low, declined, same precedent as before).** Same finding as round 1's #6 (§8) — now larger still after three real bug fixes plus their regression tests, which is expected and doesn't change the reasoning: splitting a Qodo-fix commit from the code+tests it fixes, mid-review, would hurt reviewability more than the line count itself. **7 new regression tests** (2 for the IDN fix — one testing `_idna_hostname()` directly against Requests' own encoding, one end-to-end proving `detonate()` pins an IDN host despite the Unicode/ASCII mismatch; 1 for the port fix, asserting `action_invalid: true` and that the page's other forms still get reported; 2 for the proxy fix — one unit-testing `_get_without_proxy_trust()` sets `trust_env = False`, one confirming the existing pin-wiring test still holds now that it patches the new helper instead of `requests.get` directly, since that name is no longer called). `tools/` 218 passed/2 skipped (was 214/2 before this round); `harness` untouched, still 19/19. Pushed to PR #37, **not merged** — awaiting this round's own Qodo re-review

2026-08-29 · T-045 (Qodo fix round) · [O3] · **Qodo's first pass on PR #60 found 1 real Bug, fixed in scope.** "Concurrent cache calls can lock" — `imports-mcp` is stateless per request (`server.py`), so it never serialized calls into the new cache itself; a concurrent request contending for SQLite's write lock could raise `sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` straight out of `_crtsh_db_execute()`, escaping `domain_intel()` entirely instead of degrading — a real gap in the exact "never raises, always degrades" contract this whole module already holds every other source (RDAP, crt.sh itself) to. **Fixed two ways:** (1) `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` on every connection plus an explicit `CRTSH_CACHE_DB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (10s, matching this file's other `*_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` constants) makes the contention itself rare — WAL lets readers proceed while a write is in flight, unlike the default rollback-journal mode. (2) The hard guarantee: `_crtsh_db_execute()` now catches `sqlite3.Error` and returns `[]`, the same shape as a genuine cache miss — a locked/corrupted/unreadable cache degrades to "fetch live" (reads) or "write skipped, self-heals next call" (writes), never a raise, since the cache is a speed optimization, not a correctness-critical store. Added `test_locked_cache_database_degrades_instead_of_raising`, mocking `sqlite3.connect` itself to raise `OperationalError` and confirming `domain_intel()` still returns a normal result by falling through to the live network call. `tools/` full suite: 200 passed / 3 skipped (was 199/3 — the one new test). Re-verified `✓ Resolved` against the exact final HEAD (`dbff37e`) via the GitHub API, not assumed. **Merged into `main` as `cc699f3b2fc76a617e4c7d53392c9c3e7644c505` (PR #60)** — confirmed via GitHub (`state: MERGED`, `mergedAt`, `origin/main` fast-forwarded). T-045 complete
