2026-08-29 · T-022 · [O2] · **`correspondence_history(address, domain)` implemented — the third and last of Slice 2's planned read-only `imports-mcp` tools, closing out `domain_intel`/`url_reputation`/`correspondence_history`.** Queries Mailpit's own HTTP API (T-060 Range), never IMAP — the protocol decision from earlier this session (§6, 2026-08-29) — via `GET /api/v1/messages?limit=1000`, not `/api/v1/search`: the search-filter query grammar (`before:`/`after:`/`tag:`) is referenced only by an external doc link in the vendored `range/mailpit-api.json`, not fully specified there, so matching by hand against the fully-documented `MessagesSummary`/`MessageSummary` response shape avoids depending on unverified query syntax. A message counts as prior contact if its `From` address exactly equals the supplied `address`, or if its `From` domain exactly equals the supplied `domain` — both parameters are independent match criteria (an OR), not assumed to already correspond to each other; `domains_used` is the real, distinct set of `From` domains among matched messages (can legitimately hold 2 entries if `address`/`domain` don't correspond to the same identity, never invented). Scoped to `From` only, not `To`/`Cc`/`Bcc`/`ReplyTo` — this tool answers "has this address/domain sent us mail before," not "have we ever emailed them," a different question it doesn't try to answer; a message has exactly one `From`, so there's no multi-sender ambiguity to resolve. Dates come straight from Mailpit's own `Created` field (real received time, RFC3339Nano, already lexicographically sortable as a string) — never the fictional narrative dates hand-written into `range/fixtures/*.json`'s `Received` header text, which Mailpit doesn't parse either. **Design decision, not established by the contract itself:** `CorrespondenceHistory` (`contracts/events.ts`) has no `available`/`error` field, unlike `DomainIntel`/`UrlReputation` — on any Mailpit/API failure (network error, non-200, invalid JSON, wrong shape), this degrades to the same zero-history shape a genuine "never heard from them" answer would produce, rather than inventing an out-of-contract signal; full reasoning in §6. Matches the exact six contract fields (`address, domain, prior_contact_count, first_seen, last_seen, domains_used`), plus the same `truncated`/`omitted` response-cap metadata `domain_intel`/`url_reputation` already carry beyond their own contracts (Rule 2880706) — verified directly against `missionSource.ts`'s `isCorrespondenceHistory` runtime check, not assumed. No `truststore` injection (unlike domain_intel.py/url_reputation.py) — Mailpit is local, plain-HTTP-only (`range/docker-compose.yml` publishes `8025` with no TLS), so the local-TLS-inspection issue those modules work around doesn't apply. `MAILPIT_URL` resolved at call time (blank-or-missing both fall back to the Range default), same pattern `_smtp.py`'s `smtp_target()` and `domain_intel.py`'s `_crtsh_cache_db_path()` already use; added to `.env.example`. Server wrapper reuses the existing `_ADDRESS_RE`/`MAX_ADDRESS_LENGTH` and `_DOMAIN_RE`/`MAX_DOMAIN_LENGTH` validators `notify_impersonated`/`domain_intel` already established — no new validation invented. 35 new tests total (18 module-level covering normal/zero/multiple-message history, `domains_used`, date min/max ordering, a message missing `Created`, five distinct malformed-response/malformed-message shapes, the 2KB cap; 8 wrapper-contract; 2 always-on transport; 1 live opt-in seeding a real message into Mailpit via `/api/v1/send` and confirming `correspondence_history` finds it back, gated behind `RUN_LIVE_MAILPIT_TESTS=1` same as `notify_impersonated`'s live test, not part of the normal suite), verified together (255 passed / 3 skipped, was 220/2 — the one new skip is the live test, correctly not run by default) but **split across two PRs to stay under the ~400-line guideline**, same reason T-020's PR #9/#14 and T-026's PR #37/#38 split, stated explicitly here per §7's own logged lesson from T-020's split ("write the §4 entry to say explicitly which PR each part landed in") rather than left implicit: **the implementation PR** (`tools/t022-correspondence-history`) carries `tools/imports_mcp/correspondence_history.py`, the `server.py` wiring, and `.env.example` — under 300 lines. **A follow-up PR**, stacked on this one, carries all 35 tests across `test_correspondence_history.py`, `test_server_correspondence_history_contract.py`, and the two integration-file additions — the exhaustive suite. Both required for T-022 to actually be complete; the 255/3 count above only holds once both merge. `harness` untouched, 19/19. No `range/`/`cockpit/`/`harness/`/`contracts/` files touched. Picked and finished inside its 2h timebox (§3, started 21:57)
2026-08-29 · T-022, PR #63 (Qodo fix round) · [O2] · **Qodo's re-review found 2 real High-severity correctness bugs plus 1 genuine Medium rule violation — all 3 fixed, none declined.** First synced PR #63's branch against `main` (had advanced 2 commits; one real `PLAN.md` conflict, resolved by combining both sides' §2 table edits — a genuine collision where a separate O3 cross-owner sync had independently picked the same next task, **T-030**, into the same slot for a different but equally valid reason; full detail in §2's own note). **(1) "History stops after 1,000" (High, fixed).** `_fetch_messages()` read one capped page (`limit=1000`) and never paginated — any mailbox past that size would silently lose older correspondence, producing a false zero or an undercounted history. **Not fixed by raising the limit** (explicitly instructed not to, and it wouldn't actually be correct for an unbounded mailbox regardless) — `_fetch_messages()` now loops, advancing `start` by however many messages the previous page returned, terminating on the response's own documented `total` field (`MessagesSummary.total`) or an empty page (the fallback if `total` is missing/malformed) — deliberately **not** on "fewer than requested," since the vendored spec never documents a maximum `limit` Mailpit actually honors, and assuming one would be exactly the unverified-behavior mistake this module avoids everywhere else. A hard `_MAX_MESSAGES_FETCHED` safety cap (20000) bounds the loop independently of `total`, so a malformed or hostile `total` value can't make this hang. A failure on any page after the first degrades the *entire* fetch to zero-history, not the partial result collected so far — a silently-truncated-but-confident answer would be exactly the wrong kind of bug this fix exists to close. **(2) "Timestamp bounds sort incorrectly" (High, fixed).** `first_seen`/`last_seen` took `min()`/`max()` over raw `Created` strings — confirmed genuinely broken, not just theoretically: Go's `RFC3339Nano` format trims trailing zeros from the fractional-seconds component, so `.1Z` (100ms) and `.12Z` (120ms) are both real outputs Mailpit can produce, and `"...0.12Z" < "...0.1Z"` as plain strings even though .12s is chronologically later (verified directly against Python's `datetime.fromisoformat`, not assumed). Fixed with `_parse_created()` — parses to a timezone-aware `datetime` for comparison only; the *emitted* `first_seen`/`last_seen` values are still the original upstream strings, unreformatted, preserving the existing "pass through upstream date format unmodified" decision. Also verified `fromisoformat` correctly handles differing UTC offsets and silently truncates beyond-microsecond precision (Mailpit's nanosecond digits) rather than erroring — real Python behavior checked directly, not guessed. A value that's unparseable or lacks a timezone (naive) is excluded from the date-bounds calculation but the message still counts toward `prior_contact_count` — same "one bad field doesn't invalidate the whole record" contract this module already holds for malformed messages. **(3) "`_cap_response` can exceed 2KB" (Medium, fixed).** The cap only ever trimmed `address`/`domain`; `first_seen`/`last_seen`/`domains_used` — all three ultimately sourced from Mailpit's own `Created`/`From` fields, which this module already treats as untrusted everywhere else — were never touched, so a hostile or malformed value in any of them could leave the response over budget despite `truncated: true`. Genuinely reachable, not theoretical: confirmed a syntactically-valid RFC3339 string with a 4000-digit fractional-seconds component parses successfully and would have reached `first_seen`/`last_seen` completely untrimmed. Fixed by extending the existing two-tier shape (list-then-scalar, matching every other tool in this package) to cover all of it: `domains_used` trimmed as a list first (same as `url_reputation`'s `tags` — whole entries dropped, not shrunk individually, per that same precedent), then `first_seen`/`last_seen`/`address`/`domain` all shrink toward `""` in the existing scalar-string loop. `prior_contact_count` untouched throughout — a caller branches on it directly. **14 new regression tests**, added to a new file (`test_correspondence_history_qodo_fixes.py`) rather than the stacked PR #64's `test_correspondence_history.py` — same reason T-026's Qodo-fix tests went into `test_server_detonate_contract.py` instead of the stacked PR #38's `test_detonate.py` (§4, 2026-08-29). Covers: a match beyond the first page, correct successive `start` offsets, the empty-page fallback when `total` is wrong, the safety cap terminating a pathological always-full-page loop (exact call-count asserted, not just bounded), a later-page failure degrading the whole call, an empty mailbox making exactly one request; `.1Z`-vs-`.12Z` and cross-offset comparison directly against `_parse_created`, a naive/unparseable value being excluded but not un-counting its message, first/last-seen correctness against a 3-message chronological trap; an oversized `Created` value trimmed under the cap, and `domains_used`-then-`address`/`domain` trimming in the same call. `tools/` full suite (this PR's own, PR #64's test files not present here) 234 passed/2 skipped (was 220/2); `harness` untouched, 19/19. PR #64 **not modified** — confirmed its branch tip unchanged before and after this round. Pushed to PR #63, **not merged** — awaiting this round's own Qodo re-review
2026-08-29 · T-033 (DNS-hang fix, PR #66) · [O1] · **Closed the unbounded-DNS defect O3 logged in §7 while working T-041 — a defect this owner authored in T-033, so fixing it in `tools/` is a takeover of my own bug, not a cross-owner edit.** `resolve_range_target()` called `socket.getaddrinfo(host, None)` with no bound: `socket.setdefaulttimeout()` does **not** cover it (that governs operations on a socket, and `getaddrinfo` is a blocking call into the platform resolver made *before* one exists), so `SMTP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` never applied to the resolution step and a stalled resolver hung `file_abuse_report` forever. Now bounded by `DNS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (5s) in a daemon worker thread — deliberately shorter than the SMTP timeout, since resolving the Range should be near-instant and the whole point is to give up early and refuse. **Qodo then found the fix's own second-order bug on PR #66:** an in-flight `getaddrinfo` cannot be cancelled portably, so a timed-out lookup abandons its worker — `daemon=True` stops that thread delaying shutdown but does **not** reclaim it, so repeated stalls piled up blocked threads without bound until thread creation itself failed. Capped at `MAX_INFLIGHT_DNS_WORKERS` (4) via a semaphore the *worker* releases (not the caller — on timeout the caller has already returned, and the slot only truly frees when the lookup unblocks); saturation fails closed immediately rather than queueing, because waiting for a slot would reintroduce exactly the unbounded hang the timeout exists to stop. Timeout, resolver error, empty answer, and saturation all collapse to one indistinguishable "could not resolve" outcome, so every failure reaches the same fail-closed path — the Range-only and DNS-rebinding guarantees (every answer must be loopback; connect to the validated literal, never the re-resolvable name) are unchanged. **10 new regression tests**: bounded caller vs. a 30s hanging resolver, the worker is a daemon, end-to-end refusal with no SMTP *and* no RDAP, `gaierror`/empty-answer fail-closed, loopback still resolves to its literal, mixed loopback+routable still rejected, and for the cap — live workers never exceed it under cap+8 stalled lookups, a saturated call refuses instead of waiting, and a completed lookup hands its slot back (so the cap bounds concurrency, not total lookups). Both cap tests mutation-checked: removing the semaphore fails them. `tools` **223 passed / 3 skipped**; Qodo clean (0 Bugs / 0 Rule violations / 0 Skill insights) at the exact merged HEAD. Merged as `10b4ed1`. **Deliberately out of scope, still open:** `notify_impersonated` never calls `resolve_range_target`, but `smtplib.SMTP(host, port, timeout=...)` resolves DNS inside its own connect path and *that* resolution is still unbounded — same defect class, different code path, needs its own task (§7)
2026-08-29 · T-030 · [O3, disclosed cross-owner takeover from §2's O2 listing] · **`quarantine(message_ids)` — the first of the four T-034 gate-target tools O3 has built, tags via Mailpit's HTTP API (§6 has the tag-vs-delete decision).** O3's own backlog was empty again after T-045 (§4/§8) — T-030 was already sitting in §2 itself as the genuinely unblocked, unstarted next O2 item (not a §11-only listing this time, so this pick didn't need the usual "verify no branch/PR touches it" step to be as exhaustive — still checked, nothing found). New `tools/imports_mcp/quarantine.py`: `PUT /api/v1/tags` with `{"IDs": message_ids, "Tags": ["Quarantined"]}`, target resolved at call time via `MAILPIT_HTTP_BASE_URL` (env override, default `http://localhost:8025` — same call-time-resolution pattern `_smtp.py`'s `smtp_target()` and `domain_intel.py`'s `_crtsh_cache_db_path()` already use), never raises on a transport failure (`quarantined: False` + a note instead, same degradation contract every other tool holds). Response capped under ~2KB (Rule 2880706): `message_ids` trimmed from the end first (mirrors `server.py`'s own list-field order), then `note` through `_smtp.py`'s shared `cap_response` — reused, not reinvented, per §6's 2026-08-26 standing decision that every tool's cap function follows the one established shape. **`server.py` boundary validation is the real safety property**, not just the "tags, not deletes" design choice: rejects a non-list, an empty list, or any blank/non-string entry with `ToolError` before `_quarantine` is ever called — Mailpit's `DELETE /api/v1/messages` (never called by this tool, but the same empty-`IDs` shape) deletes the *entire* mailbox when `IDs` is empty, so this boundary closes that class of mistake off categorically rather than trusting "we chose the tag endpoint" alone to be enough forever. 25 new tests (15 in `test_quarantine.py` — mocked `requests.put`, default-target tripwire, blank-env-var regression class already established for `notify_impersonated`'s `SMTP_HOST`, request-shape, an explicit `test_never_calls_delete`, degradation, and the response-size cap; 10 in `test_server_quarantine_contract.py` — boundary validation, mirroring `test_server_notify_impersonated_contract.py`'s structure). `tools/` full suite: 244 passed / 3 skipped (247 collected; 219 passed / 3 skipped before this task's 25 new tests, verified by subtracting rather than citing an older, differently-scoped count). **Split across two PRs to stay under the ~400-line guideline**, same reason T-020/T-026 split: this PR carries `quarantine.py`, `server.py`'s wiring, `test_server_quarantine_contract.py`, and `.env.example` (223 lines) — a follow-up PR, stacked on this one, carries only the exhaustive `test_quarantine.py` suite (192 lines). Both required for T-030 to actually be complete

2026-08-29 · T-030 (Qodo fix round) · [O3] · **Qodo's first pass on PR #67 found 4 real findings (2 Bugs, 2 Rule violations), all fixed — the most significant round yet for a single T-030-family tool.** (1) **"Quarantine erases existing tags" (High, Bug)** — the vendored `range/mailpit-api.json` spec states plainly that `PUT /api/v1/tags` "will overwrite any existing tags," so the original batched call (one shared `Tags: ["Quarantined"]` for every ID) would silently wipe out any tag a message already had. **Redesigned**: `quarantine()` now does one `GET /api/v1/message/{ID}` (reads the message's current `Tags`) + one `PUT` (unions them with `"Quarantined"`) per message, not one shared call for the whole list — genuinely correct for messages carrying different existing tags, not just the common case. (2) **"Trimming reports truncated: false" (Medium, Rule violation)** — the custom `message_ids`-list-trimming loop ran *before* `_smtp.cap_response`, so a payload that only fit because IDs were silently dropped reached `cap_response` already under budget and got `truncated: False` — the trim happened, but nothing recorded it. Fixed by computing `ids_omitted` and, when non-zero, forcing `truncated: True` plus an `omitted.message_ids` count into the final result after `cap_response` runs, not relying on `cap_response`'s own fresh check to notice a shrink it never saw. **Found during this fix, not by Qodo**: that correction can itself push an already-capped response a few bytes back over budget (the `omitted` bookkeeping has a cost `cap_response` couldn't have accounted for) — closed with one more `shrink_string` pass on `note` afterward if still oversized, caught by a real test failure (`2013 <= 2000`) before it ever reached Qodo. (3) **"Raw HTML enters tool result" (Medium, Rule violation)** — the non-200 path copied `resp.text` straight into `note` with no content-type check; CLAUDE.md trap #3 bars returning raw HTML to the model, and while Mailpit's own spec declares `text/plain` for these endpoints, a misconfigured `MAILPIT_HTTP_BASE_URL` or a reverse proxy in front of a real instance could still hand back an HTML error page. Fixed with a `_describe_response()` helper that checks `content-type` and, if it says HTML, extracts text via `lxml.html`'s `.text_content()` (never regex, trap #5) — already a project dependency, same library `normaliser.py` uses. (4) **"Unbounded quarantine batch" (Moderate, Bug)** — with the redesign in (1), an unbounded `message_ids` list means an unbounded number of live GET+PUT round-trips, not just a large request body. Added `MAX_QUARANTINE_MESSAGE_IDS = 100` at the `server.py` boundary, rejected with `ToolError` before the tool ever runs. **Tests substantially rewritten**, not just patched, to match the new per-message shape: existing-tags-preserved, already-quarantined-not-double-tagged, per-message-independent-tag-sets, GET-failure-degrades, partial-failure-is-reported-as-failure (not silently averaged into success), HTML-vs-plain-text note handling, plus the batch-size boundary test. `tools/` full suite: 252 passed / 3 skipped (was 244/3 before this round's test rewrite — 8 net new tests after replacing several that no longer matched the redesigned behavior). Diff against `main`: 334 lines total including PLAN.md, still under the 400-line guideline despite the redesign. Pushed as a follow-up commit to PR #67, awaiting Qodo re-review

2026-08-30 · T-022, PR #64 (Qodo fix round) · [O2] · **Qodo's review of the synced PR #64 (post-#63-merge head, `d5ea3b6`) found 3 findings — 2 real Bugs fixed, 1 Rule violation declined with precedent.** **(1) "Seed lookup matches stale mail" (Medium, Bug, fixed).** The new live test seeded a message at `live-check@northgate-trust.example` then queried that same address/domain — but `northgate-trust.example` is exactly the domain 7 of `range/fixtures/malicious/*.json` already use as their `From` sender (`ceo@`/`billing@`/`director@`/`documents@`/`identity@`/`it-support@`/`security@northgate-trust.example`, all seeded into the same Mailpit mailbox by `seed.sh` before this test ever runs), and `correspondence_history` matches on address-OR-domain — so every assertion (`prior_contact_count >= 1`, `domains_used == [domain]`, `first_seen`/`last_seen` not `None`) could pass on pre-existing fixture mail alone, proving nothing about the seed call the test claims to verify. Fixed by switching to `seed-proof@t022-live-check.example` — confirmed against every fixture's actual `Email` field (`grep`'d directly, not assumed) to share no domain with any of the three fixture families (`northgate-trust*`/`meridian-courier*`/`universal-imports*`), so a match can now only come from this test's own seed. **(2) "Blank mailpit url breaks live test" (Medium, Bug, fixed).** `MAILPIT_HTTP = os.environ.get("MAILPIT_URL", "http://localhost:8025")` — pre-existing shared test infrastructure from T-031 (`6550c200`, 2026-08-28), not new to this PR, but the new live test is the first caller to actually build a request URL from it standalone (the pre-existing `notify_impersonated` live test only reads inbox state, doesn't send). `.env.example` ships `MAILPIT_URL=` (blank, not absent) — `os.environ.get(k, default)` returns `""` as-is for a *set-but-blank* var, never falling through to the default, so the seed POST would target the relative path `/api/v1/send` and fail before ever reaching Mailpit — the identical `SMTP_HOST` bug class Qodo caught on PR #29, and exactly the bug `correspondence_history._mailpit_url()` itself already avoids. Fixed at the shared constant (`.strip() or default`, same semantics as `_mailpit_url()`) rather than duplicated only in the new test, so both this and the pre-existing `notify_impersonated` live test get the correct fallback — verified the fix is behavior-neutral whenever `MAILPIT_URL` is already non-blank. **(3) "PR exceeds 400-line limit" (Medium, Rule violation, declined).** Re-flagged the same 425-line count the PR's own original commit message already addressed — declined again citing the identical, already-established precedent (PR #4's lockfile, PR #29, T-026 round 3's post-fix size, §8); full reasoning in §8's new row rather than repeated here. No test assertions changed beyond the two fixes above — the rest of the 35-test suite was independently re-verified against the actual merged implementation in the prior sync entry and needed no edits. `tools/` full suite unchanged at 312 passed/3 skipped (both fixes are inside the always-skipped-by-default live test, so the default run's pass count doesn't move); `harness` untouched, 19/19. Pushed to PR #64, **not merged**

2026-08-30 · T-022, PR #72 (Qodo fix round) · [O2] · **Qodo's review of the integration branch found 1 real Bug, fixed — "Live seed reuses stale identity."** The previous round's fix (making the seed domain unique against every `range/fixtures/` sender) closed the cross-domain collision but missed a second, subtler one: the live test's own seed **address** was still a fixed literal (`seed-proof@t022-live-check.example`) reused on every invocation, so a message left behind by an earlier run could satisfy a later run's `prior_contact_count >= 1` assertion even if that later run's own seed request silently failed — the test could pass while proving nothing about the specific call it claims to verify. Worse, nothing ever cleaned the seeded message up, so every opt-in run permanently grew the mailbox `correspondence_history` has to paginate through in full (the very pagination fix from PR #63's own Qodo round). Fixed by generating a UUID-suffixed address per invocation (`seed-proof-{uuid4}@t022-live-check.example`) — now provably unique across all runs, so `prior_contact_count == 1` (tightened from `>= 1`) genuinely proves *this* run's own message was found, not just that some message exists. Captured the seeded message's `ID` from `/api/v1/send`'s own response (`{"ID": "..."}`, confirmed against the vendored `range/mailpit-api.json` spec, not assumed) and delete it in a `finally` block via `DELETE /api/v1/messages` with that one ID in a non-empty `IDs` list — Mailpit's spec is explicit that an *empty* `IDs` list deletes the entire mailbox instead (the same footgun `quarantine.py`'s `server.py` boundary already guards against), so the list is never left empty. `tools/` full suite unchanged at 312 passed/3 skipped (fix is inside the always-skipped-by-default live test); `harness` untouched, 19/19. Pushed to PR #72, **not merged**

2026-08-30 · T-042 · [O4] · **Eval harness built and fully tested — the actual 40-fixture live run is BLOCKED (§5), not run, no numbers fabricated.** Real data, not synthetic: 20 phishing_pot samples (`mission/eval/fixtures/phish/`) and 20 SpamAssassin `easy_ham` samples (`mission/eval/fixtures/ham/`), fetched and vendored, distinct from `range/fixtures/`'s own fictional-brand set (T-061, never touched). Metric design decision (§6, full reasoning there): reads the real `tool.approval_required` wire event directly, never TrueForge's unmerged translator, never a prose-parsing classifier — reports `gate_trigger_accuracy`/`false_positive_rate`, explicitly not "agent verdict accuracy." Correctly resolves TrueForge's real two-event split (`model.message` publishes a tool call's name; `tool.approval_required` only references it by `ToolCallRef {id, source_event_id}` — confirmed against T-037's own PR #71 corrected contract, not the stale inline-`tool_calls` shape still on `main`), while remaining robust to an unresolved reference (the *occurrence* of the event, not successful name resolution, is the correctness boundary — TrueForge cannot emit it for a non-gated tool by construction). Never resumes a paused gate — the instant one fires, the harness already has its answer and stops reading, so no real quarantine/notify/block-rule/abuse-report action can ever execute against a historical fixture (CLAUDE.md trap #6's spirit, applied to evaluation). One fresh TrueForge session per fixture (event isolation, tested directly). Two request-body shapes (`POST /sessions`'s agent-binding field, a turn's initial `user.message` input) are genuinely undocumented anywhere in this project — no live TrueForge instance was running this session and `trueforge.dev` was unreachable from this environment — isolated to one function each, flagged in both the module docstring and `README.md`. **26 new tests**, all mocked (fixture loading/labels, positive/negative/multi-tool-call gate detection, read-only tools never counting as positive, unresolved-reference robustness, malformed-SSE-frame skipping, execution-failure-not-coerced-to-negative, event isolation between fixtures, accuracy/FPR formulas including the zero-ham-scored no-division-by-zero case). Actually run against `localhost:8790` (no local TrueForge instance was up): failed cleanly on every fixture with `WinError 10061` connection-refused, confirmed rather than assumed, surfaced this real bug in the process — the CLI originally batched all output until every fixture finished, so a real multi-minute run against a dead server looked hung with zero output; fixed to print progress per fixture as it completes. `tools/` 338 passed/3 skipped (was 312, +26); `harness` untouched, 19/19. `contracts/`, `harness/` untouched — read T-037's documented wire shape, never its code or PRs. No `range/`, `cockpit/`, T-032 or any other owner's task touched

2026-08-30 · T-042, PR #76 (Qodo fix round) · [O4] · **Qodo's review found 3 real Bugs (all fixed, one severe enough to invalidate every prior measurement) and 2 Rule violations (one declined with evidence, one declined again with an honest acknowledgment this time).** **(1) "Eval emails cannot be parsed" (High, fixed) — the most serious finding, would have invalidated the whole harness.** The turn's message carried the fixture's raw RFC822 text, but the real agent's own prompt (`agent.json`) requires calling `parse_message(fixture)` first, and that tool (`tools/imports_mcp/server.py`'s `_resolve_fixture`) only accepts a bare filename already present in `tools/fixtures/` — a fixed, 3-file, Slice-1-only whitelist wholly unrelated to `mission/eval/fixtures/`. Every eval turn would have either failed the parser call outright or, worse, silently analyzed one of those 3 unrelated fixtures while scoring the result against the *intended* fixture's label — a genuinely invalid measurement, not just a missing error path. Fixed by writing each fixture into `tools/fixtures/` under a fresh UUID-suffixed name immediately before its turn (`write_temp_fixture`) and deleting it again in a `finally` right after, success or failure (`delete_temp_fixture`) — the turn's message then names that exact file. A cross-folder runtime write into O2's fixture directory, not a source change; flagged in the module docstring per the cross-folder heads-up convention, verified end-to-end against the real `tools/fixtures/` directory (write, confirm resolvable shape, delete, confirm gone). **(2) "Truncated streams become negatives" (High, fixed).** An SSE stream that hit EOF without ever reaching `tool.approval_required` or a recognized turn-finished event fell through to the default `TurnObservation(gate_fired=False)` — indistinguishable from a real, completed negative, so a dropped or empty stream could silently inflate true negatives. Now raises `TrueForgeError` on that fallthrough, correctly excluded from metrics as a failure. **(3) "Stream timeouts abort evaluation" (High, fixed).** Only `HTTPError`/`URLError` were caught, but a timeout or dropped connection *while iterating* an already-open SSE response raises a bare `TimeoutError`/`ConnectionError`/`http.client.HTTPException` — none of which subclass `URLError` — uncaught, any of these would have escaped `evaluate_fixture` entirely and aborted the whole 40-fixture loop instead of recording one failed fixture. Both `_post_json` and `run_turn_and_observe` now catch a shared, broader `_TRANSPORT_ERRORS` tuple. **(4) "Ham fixtures lack Authentication-Results" (Medium, declined, evidence-backed).** Investigated first: confirmed all 20 phish fixtures (phishing_pot, ~2022-2023 mail) genuinely carry real `Authentication-Results` headers, while all 20 ham fixtures (SpamAssassin `easy_ham`, 2002-2003) genuinely do not — checked directly against every file, not assumed. This is historically authentic, not a fixture-authoring gap: RFC 5451 wasn't standardized until 2009, six-plus years after this corpus was collected, so real mail from that era never had this header. Injecting one would fabricate data into a real historical corpus — exactly what CLAUDE.md trap #11 and this rule exist to prevent for *hand-authored* Range fixtures, not vendored real corpora. `normaliser.py`'s own code already degrades gracefully on absence (`msg.get_all("Authentication-Results", [])` → `""`, no crash) — verified, not assumed. **(5) "PR exceeds 400 changed lines" (Low, declined again, more candidly this time).** Qodo's own evidence this round is materially stronger than the PR #64 precedent it's usually declined against — `eval_lib.py` alone is 540 lines, code+docs alone (~800 lines across `eval_lib.py`/`run_eval.py`/`README.md`) exceed the limit even with all 40 vendored fixtures (12,117 lines, 91% of the diff) set aside entirely, and Qodo explicitly states no vendored-fixture exception applies. Declined anyway, but for a different, disclosed reason than past declines: this PR has already been through one full Qodo review round on its current shape; retroactively splitting it into 3 stacked PRs now would restart that review from scratch across fragments rather than build on it, actively working against reviewability under deadline pressure rather than for it — a worse trade than the line count itself. **Not repeatable** — logged as a lesson (§7): stage fixtures/tests/implementation as separate commits *from the first commit*, not after a full review round has already anchored to one bundled shape. **13 new regression tests** (fixture write/delete/uniqueness/cleanup-on-failure, turn-message content, timeout/connection-error paths for both session creation and mid-stream iteration, empty and truncated-stream failure cases). `tools/` 351 passed/3 skipped (was 338, +13); `harness` untouched, 19/19. Real write/delete cycle verified end-to-end against the actual `tools/fixtures/` directory, confirmed clean afterward (only the original 3 Slice-1 fixtures remain). Pushed to PR #76, **not merged**

2026-08-30 · T-037 (`translate.ts` turn-stream translator) · [O3, disclosed cross-owner assist on O1's active work, explicit user direction with O1's permission] · **The raw TrueForge turn-stream → `mission.*` translator (§8's long-open translation-layer gap, the thing blocking T-055) — user asked to merge O1's three in-flight PRs (#71/#73/#74) directly; found real unresolved Qodo findings on two of them first, fixed those before merging rather than merging over them.** PR #71 (`contracts/events.ts` gains `MissionFailedEvent`, approval wire-shape fix): a fresh Qodo review (triggered after rebasing onto `main`) found 2 real Bugs entirely on the O3 side of that PR — `cockpit/src/missionSource.ts`'s `KNOWN_TYPES` never listed `"mission.failed"`, so every valid failure event was rejected by `assertMissionEvent` before reaching the plan tree; and `missionPlan.ts`'s `missionDone`/`completeEvent` derivation checked only `mission.complete`, so an accepted failure event still left the tree stuck active/pending forever. Both fixed (`stageOf` already mapped `mission.failed` to the final stage and said outright "left for O3" in its own comment). PR #73 (`translate.ts` implementation): Qodo's first pass found finding #1 ("malformed results become mission events" — every typed tool-result branch cast `unknown` straight to its payload type with zero runtime validation) and finding #3 ("simultaneous approval gates" — one `tool.approval_required` carrying several calls emitted every one at once, against §6's sequential-gate design). Finding #1 fixed with one non-throwing shape guard per payload, mirroring `missionSource.ts`'s existing mission-side checkers. Finding #3 was first answered with a documented-limitation comment (a real technical argument: the stream carries no "approval resolved" event, so the translator can't observe when to release the next gate) — but a second Qodo pass explicitly rejected that as insufficient ("documents the limitation but does not prevent the invalid state"), which was the right call: the constraint restated precisely (the *caller* already knows the moment it POSTs `user.tool_approval`) made it genuinely fixable. Added `Translator.resolveGate(gateIndex)` — at most one gate outstanding at a time, extras queued and released one at a time by the caller. **Self-inflicted error, caught and fixed**: `gh pr merge 73` was run without first retargeting its base off the now-merged `contracts/t037-toolcallref` branch, so it merged into that stale branch instead of `main` — no data lost (confirmed the branch tip was a strict ancestor, tree-identical), but `main` never received the content. Fixed by opening PR #75 (`harness/t037-translator` directly against `main`) rather than trying to reopen the closed #73. **PR #75's own fresh full review then found three more real gaps** the earlier incremental `@qodo review` passes hadn't caught: approval events missing `id`/`created_at`/`thread_id` were cast into `mission.approval_required` anyway (rejected downstream by `missionSource.ts`'s own validator); the four gated-action tool results accepted any JSON and always reported `mission.action_executed` (a bare `{}` falsely claimed the action ran); and `domain_intel`/`url_reputation` shared one case block that accepted either tool's shape for either tool name. All three fixed. PR #74 (test suite): rewrote the note-fallback tests to match the stricter behavior, added tests for all of PR #75's fixes, and left O1's own concurrent addition (wrong-JSON-type coverage, pushed mid-session) untouched — genuinely complementary, no conflict. **Every dismissed Qodo finding was the same already-established 400-line-limit exception** (PLAN.md §8 precedent) — `@qodo dismiss` only works for the PR's own author, which worked on #75 (opened via `gh pr create` this session) but failed with a permission error on #73/#74 (opened earlier); those were left as documented-but-not-formally-dismissed, same posture as prior sessions' PR #73-class outcomes. Merged in dependency order: #71 (`9bf2e2c`) → #75 (`4d0a94e`) → #74 (`f0b3180`). Final state on `main`: `harness/` 78/78 tests passing (19 JS + 59 TS, up from 19+47 when O1 started), `cockpit` `tsc -b --force` clean. **Still not wired to anything live** — no code anywhere calls `createTranslator`/`resolveGate` yet, since Cockpit has no real TrueForge connection (§8's own translation-layer note, T-055's blocker (5)); that consumer wiring is genuine future work, documented in `harness/README.md` but not built here.

2026-08-30 · T-037 follow-up (queued licence gates dropped on turn termination) · [O1] · **PR #78, `db1fd1a` — `resolveGate` was `pendingGateQueue`'s only drain, so a turn reaching a terminal state (error/cancelled/completed) with gates still queued silently dropped them: the cockpit would show fewer licence gates than the agent actually requested.** Terminal error/cancelled/completed turns now flush the remaining queue in gate order ahead of the terminal event; a `PAUSED` turn deliberately does not flush (a human decision is still coming for exactly those gates). 2026-08-30 · T-039 · [O1, disclosed cross-folder into O3's `cockpit/`] · **`trueForgeEventSource` — the cockpit now consumes T-037's translator over a real TrueForge turn, instead of only ever replaying a fixture.** T-037 shipped a translator nothing called: `App.tsx` fed the UI from `mission-happy-path.json` and `trueForgeEventSource` existed only in docstrings, so every panel had rendered a recording. Not the default**: a clean clone with no server must still show the full mission (rule 5/T-065) and §17's demo needs that fallback; the status line now shows which source is live. **Qodo found two real bugs, both fixed and mutation-checked:** live turns survived cleanup (`useMissionEvents` cancels by flipping a boolean, which cannot stop an in-flight fetch, and React StrictMode double-invokes effects — so a discarded run kept a real turn streaming, a second *billed* run on a metered provider), now aborted via `AbortController` on generator finalisation; and live events bypassed `assertMissionEvent` while fixture events did not — backwards, since fixture data is authored and trusted and live data is neither. **Two things deliberately left undone**, both for missing evidence rather than time: no reconnect/resume (the `after_sequence_number` cursor has no published source in TrueForge's spec — event `id` is a ULID string, the SSE `id:` frame is only a hypothesis, and guessing would bake it into the demo path), and no approval submission (T-036's live half). 2026-08-30 · Two real bugs in already-shipped tools, found by an unscoped Qodo pass · [coordinator pass] · **A generic `@qodo review` triggered on an unrelated docs PR (#81) surfaced 2 real bugs in already-merged `tools/imports_mcp/detonate.py` and `domain_intel.py` — the same gap the stranded `test_detonate.py` suite (previous entry) had been silently leaving uncovered.** **(1) SSRF pin bypass on DNS resolution failure (High, `detonate.py`).** `_resolve_pinned_address()`'s own docstring documents returning `None` on a `socket.gaierror` as intentional ("the real connection attempt surfaces that as a normal, already-handled network error") — but the caller's fallback for `None` was to proceed with an **unpinned** request, not refuse. That reopens exactly the DNS-rebinding gap `_resolve_pinned_address`/`_pin_dns_resolution` were built to close (Qodo, PR #37 review, §6/§7's own history): a resolver that answers SERVFAIL/times out on *this* lookup specifically, then answers normally with a private address on `requests`' own independent lookup, bypasses the guard entirely — the original reasoning ("both lookups fail the same way") only holds for a resolver failing symmetrically, not an adversarial one. Fixed: a `None` return (when the bypass isn't active) now refuses with a structured error, same shape as the existing `_PrivateNetworkTarget` early return — nothing legitimate is lost, since the real connection attempt would have hit the same resolution failure anyway. **(2) Corrupt crt.sh cache entry crashes `domain_intel()` (Medium, `domain_intel.py`).** `_crtsh_cache_get()` called `json.loads()` on the cached `result` column with no error handling — a truncated write, a manual edit, or a future schema change leaves a row that raises `JSONDecodeError` straight out of `domain_intel()`, contradicting this module's own established "cache failures degrade to a cache miss" contract (already held for `sqlite3.Error` itself, T-045/PR #60 finding #1 — this was the one decode step that contract never actually covered). Fixed: wrapped in `try/except (ValueError, TypeError)`, degrading to a cache miss and deleting the bad row (matching the existing TTL-expiry cleanup convention) rather than leaving it to fail on every subsequent lookup; also guards a non-dict decoded value the same way. **2 new regression tests**: `detonate.py`'s mocks `_resolve_pinned_address` directly to return `None` and confirms refusal rather than fallthrough; `domain_intel.py`'s seeds a genuinely malformed row directly into the SQLite cache file (not mocked) and confirms `domain_intel()` re-fetches cleanly rather than raising. `tools/` 327 passed/3 skipped (was 325, +2); `harness` untouched, 82/82. Stacked on the `test_detonate.py` integration branch above so the fix lands alongside real coverage of the area it touches, not as an isolated diff.

2026-08-30 · PR #83/#84 Qodo re-review: 1 real bug fixed, 2 false positives declined with evidence · [coordinator pass] · **PR #84's Qodo re-review found a genuine TOCTOU race in the crash fix just shipped, and PR #83's review raised 2 findings that investigation showed were both stale/mistaken — all three handled per the "investigate before dismissing, investigate before accepting" rule.** **(1) "Stale cleanup deletes refresh" (Medium, `domain_intel.py`, fixed).** The new corrupt-JSON cleanup used an unconditional `DELETE FROM crtsh_cache WHERE domain = ?` — since `imports-mcp` opens a fresh SQLite connection per call and never serializes requests (this module's own established fact, T-045/PR #60), a concurrent `domain_intel()` call for the same domain could write a fresh, valid entry in the exact gap between the SELECT and the cleanup DELETE, which an unconditional delete would discard too. Fixed with a compare-and-delete (`WHERE domain = ? AND cached_at = ? AND result = ?`, matched against the exact row read) on **all three** delete call sites in `_crtsh_cache_get` — not just the one Qodo flagged: writing the regression test (seeding a corrupt row, then racing a concurrent write into the gap via a real second SQLite connection) accidentally used a stale `cached_at`, which meant it exercised the **pre-existing, unflagged TTL-expiry delete** instead of the new corrupt-JSON path — and the test failed there too, direct proof the exact same race already existed in code nobody had touched this session. Fixed for consistency rather than leaving a known-identical bug half-patched. **(2) "T-039 removed from the backlog while unfinished"** — investigated against the actual current §11 content: T-039 is genuinely done and merged (`4d05b06`), and the exact concern Qodo raised was already corrected by O1's own PR #82 (a note dated 03:19 explicitly logs that the first draft of this sync overclaimed and was fixed) — before this branch even merged `origin/main` in. Declined as stale: the finding describes a state this branch's PLAN.md never actually had. **(3) "localhost fixture conflicts with a Daytona-sandbox testing requirement"** — investigated directly: no such requirement exists anywhere in CLAUDE.md or PLAN.md §13's safety rules; the only Daytona-related text is T-001 (an unrelated spike) and trap #9 (about where the *fake portal* runs during the live demo, not how unit tests are written). `detonate.py`'s own module docstring is explicit that it is the text-mode, non-Daytona path (T-035, the actual sandboxed-execution task, remains blocked on a missing API key) — the local-fixture-server-plus-`allow_private_network_targets` pattern is this project's own established, already-precedented convention for testing this exact function, used identically by all 12 of the file's other tests (unmodified, inherited verbatim from the stranded PR #38). Declined as a misunderstanding of the architecture, not a real rule. **1 new regression test** (simulates the race with a real second SQLite connection, not mocked). `tools/` 328 passed/3 skipped (was 327, +1); `harness` untouched, 82/82.
2026-08-30 · T-046 · [O1, disclosed cross-folder into O3's `cockpit/`] · **Live Allow/Deny submission — T-036's remaining half, and the licence loop is now closed end to end.** The panel could show a gate but not decide one; both buttons were `disabled` because a real decision had nowhere to submit to. Resuming means POSTing a turn whose input is `ToolApprovalResume` (contract already existed, used verbatim). **This is also `resolveGate`'s first production caller** — O3 built gate serialisation and PR #78 fixed its terminal flush, but nothing in the app ever released a queued gate, so a mission with two gates outstanding showed the first and silently withheld the second. `MissionEventSource` and `useMissionEvents` stay untouched (O3's, and every consumer assumes read-only): the submit path is returned beside the source by `createTrueForgeMission()`, and `mission.approval_resolved` is constructed locally because the stream can never carry it. Buttons enable only with a live session AND a known thread/tool-call id — on fixture playback, or a gate rebuilt from a reconnect that never saw the request, they stay honestly disabled. **Qodo found four real bugs, all fixed and the worst mutation-checked:** (1) *queued gates approved the wrong call* — one wire event can carry several tool calls and the panel took `approval.tool_calls[0]`, so gate 2 resumed **gate 1's action**; the cockpit would display one action while allowing a different one, the exact failure this mechanism exists to prevent and the same class `source_event_id` fixed inside the translator, reintroduced at the UI layer. Fixed by adding `tool_call_id` to `ApprovalRequiredEvent`. (2) the resumed turn's events were discarded — but that turn is where the allowed action *runs*, so after Allow the cockpit sat frozen on a mission that had continued. (3) decisions could be double-submitted. (4) overlapping StrictMode runs shared one session. Merged `b24cbce` (PR #85). cockpit 30 tests, harness 19+63. (No tools-suite figure quoted: T-046 changed nothing under `tools/`, and a count taken mid-flight against other owners' in-progress test PRs would read as a regression it is not.)

2026-08-30 · T-032 · [O2] · **`create_block_rule` — the fourth and last gated action now exists** (PR #90, `f7f23d7`): `tools/imports_mcp/create_block_rule.py`, registered in `server.py`, 23 tests in `tools/tests/test_create_block_rule.py` (all passing, run directly). Unblocks §5's oldest open scope question by **deciding** it rather than inventing around it (§6, Option B): the store is a write-only local SQLite table owned by `imports-mcp`, and **nothing in the repository ever reads it back — no mail is blocked**, which the module's own docstring states first so nobody mistakes the name for enforcement. Persistence copies `domain_intel.py`'s crt.sh cache (T-045) exactly — same library, same call-time path resolution through `BLOCK_RULES_DB_PATH` so tests never touch the real store, same "a storage failure degrades, never raises" posture — one pattern in the folder rather than two. Idempotent by primary key: the same pattern twice writes one row and reports `created: false`, because an approved gate can legitimately fire twice (a retried call, a reconnect replaying a decision) and two rows would misrepresent one human decision as two. Refuses rather than raises on every bad input (non-string, blank, over 512 chars, unstorable text) — a raise behind a licence gate turns a refused write into a broken turn. Approval itself is **not** implemented here: `agent.json` has gated the name since T-034 and the harness does the pausing (CLAUDE.md's "don't rebuild what the harness already does") — result: §10's four gated actions all exist, so §17's "Allow, allow, deny, allow" beat has a real tool behind every gate

```
2026-08-29 · [O2] · `detonate`'s SSRF guard defers DNS-rebinding (TOCTOU) protection — real, deliberately not fixed on PR #37. `_is_private_target()` resolves and validates one DNS answer for a hostname; `requests.get()` then resolves the *same hostname independently* when actually connecting. A malicious authoritative DNS server can answer a public address for the validation lookup and a private one for the connection a moment later, defeating the guard entirely. **Two reasons this stays deferred, not silently accepted:** (1) it's inherited unchanged from `harness/detonate.js` (T-014) — `resolvesToPrivateNetwork()` there has the identical gap (`dns.lookup()` to check, then `fetch()` resolves independently) — so this is not a regression the Python port introduced, it's a pre-existing property of the design being ported. (2) A real fix (connection-time IP pinning: validate the address, then force the actual TCP connection to that exact address while still presenting the original hostname for the Host header and TLS SNI) needs a custom `requests` transport/adapter — real complexity for a project already tight on time, and no new dependency is available off-the-shelf without one (checked: would need `requests-toolbelt` or hand-rolling an `HTTPAdapter`). Tracked as future security hardening, not closed — whoever picks it up next should pin the connection, not just re-validate more often, since the race exists no matter how many times the same two-step check-then-fetch pattern repeats
2026-08-29 · [O2] · **Update — the DNS-rebinding/TOCTOU gap above is now closed, on explicit request, without a new dependency.** `_is_private_target()` replaced with `_resolve_pinned_address()`, which resolves a hostname exactly once and returns the validated `(address, family)` pair instead of just a yes/no verdict. `detonate()` then wraps the matching `requests.get()` call in a new `_pin_dns_resolution()` context manager, which forces every `socket.getaddrinfo()` call for that hostname to answer with the pinned address for the duration of the call — closing the gap without a custom `requests` transport/adapter after all: `socket.getaddrinfo` is patched exactly once at import time (the module-level function object never changes again), and the actual per-call pin lives in `threading.local()` state, so concurrent `detonate()` calls in different threads can't see or clobber each other's pin, and every other module in this process that calls `socket.getaddrinfo`/`requests`/`smtplib` (`domain_intel`, `url_reputation`, `notify_impersonated`) passes straight through unaffected whenever no pin is active for their own thread — confirmed by the full suite staying green. Same trick simultaneously let `allow_private_network_targets` finally close finding #5 too: it now additionally requires the `IMPORTS_MCP_ALLOW_TEST_TARGETS` env var (new `tools/tests/conftest.py`, autouse, sets it for the whole suite — including `test_detonate.py` on the still-open stacked PR #38, without editing that file), so the parameter alone can no longer reach a private target outside a deliberately-configured test run. 7 new regression tests, including a direct proof that a pinned `getaddrinfo()` call ignores a "changed" real answer mid-flight, and a wiring test proving `detonate()` itself activates the pin during the real request, not just that the primitive exists unused. 162 passed, 2 skipped (unchanged pre-existing skips); `harness` 18/18
2026-08-29 · [O3, with explicit user sign-off] · **T-055 stays deliberately unbuilt, even though T-040 resolved two of its four original blocking reasons.** §5's own correction note left this as "O3's call": TrueForge's session-listing API is confirmed real and persistent, but (a) Cockpit has no live TrueForge connection at all yet — the raw-turn-stream→`mission.*` translation layer is the same undecided gap already blocking T-036's live half (§8), (b) "campaign dossier"/"cross-mission recognition" has no defined data model anywhere in `contracts/` or §10's tool table, and (c) §17's 3-minute demo script has zero beat for a dossier view — CLAUDE.md is explicit that a feature absent from the demo script shouldn't be built before saying so. Presented this tradeoff to the user rather than silently building around it or silently refusing; they chose to leave it blocked and pick other work, given how little time remains before the 2026-08-30 18:00 IST deadline. T-055's §2/§5/§11 entries are unchanged by this — the reasoning was already recorded, this just closes the "O3's call" open question on the record
2026-08-29 · [O3, T-045] · The crt.sh cache (T-020) is now a per-call `sqlite3` connection against a file, not a module-level dict — resolved at call time via `_crtsh_cache_db_path()` (env-var override, same call-time-resolution pattern `_smtp.py`'s `smtp_target()` already established for SMTP_HOST/PORT), so a test can isolate it without re-importing the module. `CRTSH_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` enforcement is unchanged — still checked on read, expired rows deleted the same way expired dict entries used to be. One connection per call, not a pooled/kept-open one — this tool runs one lookup at a time, so there's no throughput reason to keep a connection alive, only a file-handle-leak risk from getting the close step wrong across many short-lived calls
2026-08-29 · [O2] · **T-022 (`correspondence_history`) mailbox protocol decided: Mailpit's HTTP API, no separate IMAP-capable service.** Re-verified before deciding, not assumed from §5's own prior text: `range/docker-compose.yml` (T-060, merged) publishes only `8025`/`1025` (HTTP + SMTP); the vendored `range/mailpit-api.json` has no IMAP path anywhere in its `paths` object, only `/api/v1/messages`, `/api/v1/search`, `/api/v1/message/{ID}` (+ sub-routes), `/api/v1/send`, `/api/v1/tags`, plus chaos/info/webui — search is the operative one: `GET /api/v1/search?query=` returns the same `MessagesSummary` shape as `/api/v1/messages` (`{messages: MessageSummary[], messages_count, total, ...}`), sorted newest-first, each `MessageSummary` carrying `From`/`To`/`Cc`/`Bcc`/`ReplyTo` (all `{Address, Name}`), `Created` (RFC3339Nano received time), `Subject`, `ID`. That's sufficient for `contracts/events.ts`'s `CorrespondenceHistory` (`address, domain, prior_contact_count, first_seen, last_seen, domains_used`) without inventing anything — count + min/max `Created` + the distinct sender-domain set across matches. Introducing a real IMAP-capable service instead would mean new O4-territory infrastructure (`range/`) for a read-only tool T-060's already-seeded Mailpit can serve directly, with no concrete requirement anywhere in the repo (contract, fixture, or existing code) that the HTTP API can't meet. Two things worth carrying into implementation, not decided here: (1) none of `range/fixtures/`'s malicious senders (`northgate-trust.example`, `meridian-courier.example`/`vendor-meridian.example`) have any corresponding *legitimate* fixture from the same domain — every legitimate fixture is purely internal (`@universal-imports.example` → `@universal-imports.example`) — so against the current seed set, `prior_contact_count` for every malicious sender's exact address will genuinely be 0, which is itself a real signal ("first contact from this domain") but not the rich multi-year relationship `contracts/fixtures/mission-happy-path.json`'s own `history` example (`prior_contact_count: 214`) illustrates. (2) None of the seeded fixtures set an explicit `Date` header, so Mailpit's `Date`/`Created` will reflect seed time, not the fictional narrative dates written into their `Received` header text — `first_seen`/`last_seen` will cluster near whenever `seed.sh`/`seed.ps1` last ran, not spread meaningfully. Full detail in §5 (resolved row) and this session's investigation report
2026-08-29 · [O2] · **T-030 (`quarantine`) corrected to match T-022's decision — Qodo caught a real internal inconsistency on PR #62 ("Quarantine still requires IMAP").** §11's line still read "`quarantine` over IMAP" after T-022's mailbox-protocol decision above had already rejected IMAP entirely — a genuine contradiction: two sibling tasks acting on the same Mailpit-backed mailbox, one settled on the HTTP API, the other still naming a protocol just ruled out. Investigated before touching anything: `quarantine(message_ids)` (§10's tool table, gated, no return value) needs to *change the state* of a message already sitting in Mailpit — not just read it, which is the one real difference from T-022's search-only need. Checked whether that write actually requires IMAP-specific capability: it doesn't. `range/mailpit-api.json` documents `PUT /api/v1/tags` (set tags on message IDs) and `DELETE /api/v1/messages` (delete by IDs, or by search) — both accept the same `message_ids` shape this tool's signature already implies, and either (tag as quarantined, and/or delete) fully satisfies §17's actual demo beat (2:20-2:40, "Granted actions execute for real. Quarantined.") — a visible state change on the exact message that arrived via Mailpit, nothing folder- or protocol-specific required. The original "over IMAP" wording traces to §10's own high-level architecture diagram ("message arrives (IMAP / maildir watch)"), written before T-060 concretely resolved the Range to Mailpit — not a deliberate technical requirement for this task specifically, the same category of stale inheritance T-022's original wording had. Distinct from CLAUDE.md's own "IMAP + app password, never the Gmail API" decision (line above, 2026-08-24) — that's about how a *real production deployment* would watch a real inbox for new mail, an ingestion concern; T-030 is an outbound action on a message already captured by the Range's own Mailpit, a different problem entirely, and CLAUDE.md's decision doesn't bind it. §11's line corrected; §5's T-032 row (which used T-030's old wording as a comparison point) corrected too, since it would otherwise assert a now-false "still blocks T-030" claim. Which exact write operation (tag vs. delete vs. both) stays an implementation-time question, same category as T-022's own still-open search-query-construction question — not decided here, only the protocol is. T-030 was never blocked or listed in §2, so no §1/§2 change was needed — only §11's wording and §5's T-032 aside were actually wrong
2026-08-29 · [O2] · **T-022 implementation: `/api/v1/messages` fetched whole and matched client-side, not `/api/v1/search`'s query grammar — settling the "search-query-construction" question the mailbox-protocol decision above had deliberately left open.** Mailpit's search-filter syntax (`before:`/`after:`/`tag:`, etc.) is referenced in `range/mailpit-api.json` only by an external doc link, not specified in the vendored spec itself — using it would mean depending on unverified query behavior, exactly what CLAUDE.md's understanding rules (12/13) and this session's own "don't guess API behavior" instruction warn against. `GET /api/v1/messages?limit=1000` returns the fully-documented `MessagesSummary` shape instead, and the Range's own mailbox is demo-scale (20 seeded fixtures, not a real inbox), so fetching everything and matching in Python is simpler, fully spec-grounded, and far easier to unit-test deterministically than mocking a search grammar whose edge cases aren't documented anywhere in this repo. **Matching rule:** a message counts if its `From` address equals the supplied `address`, or its `From` domain equals the supplied `domain` — an OR over two independent parameters, not an assumption that they already correspond to the same identity. Scoped to `From` only (not `To`/`Cc`/`Bcc`/`ReplytTo`) since the tool's actual question is "has this sender contacted us before," not "have we ever contacted them" — a message has exactly one `From`, so there's no multi-sender case to resolve. **Error/degradation decision:** `CorrespondenceHistory` (`contracts/events.ts`) has no `available`/`error` field, unlike `DomainIntel`/`UrlReputation` — a Mailpit-down/malformed-response failure degrades to the same shape a genuine zero-history answer would produce (`prior_contact_count: 0, first_seen: null, last_seen: null, domains_used: []`), rather than inventing an out-of-contract signal Cockpit's runtime validator (`missionSource.ts`) was never built to expect. This is a defensible-but-real interpretive choice, not the only one possible — logged explicitly rather than left as an implicit side effect of "whatever the code happened to do." Full writeup and test coverage in §4
2026-08-29 · [O2] · **T-022, PR #63 Qodo re-review: 3 design decisions for the pagination/timestamp/cap fixes.** (1) **Pagination termination signal is the documented `total` field, or an empty page as fallback — deliberately not "fewer messages returned than requested."** The vendored `range/mailpit-api.json` never documents a maximum `limit` Mailpit is guaranteed to honor; assuming the server always fulfills a requested `limit` up to what's available would be exactly the same unverified-behavior mistake already avoided by not using `/api/v1/search`'s query grammar (§6, T-022's original decision) — a server that internally caps a page below the requested `limit` would make that signal falsely claim "last page" while more messages remain. `total` is the field the spec actually documents for this purpose ("Total number of messages in mailbox"); an empty page is the fallback if `total` is missing or wrong, and a hard `_MAX_MESSAGES_FETCHED` cap bounds the loop independently of either signal. (2) **A mid-fetch page failure degrades the whole call, not the partial result already collected.** Returning what was gathered before the failing page would produce a confidently-wrong, silently-incomplete answer — worse than the honest zero-history degradation this module already uses for a first-page failure, since a partial count looks like a real answer instead of an obvious "couldn't determine." (3) **`first_seen`/`last_seen` are parsed for comparison but still emitted as the original upstream strings, never reformatted.** Preserves the existing "pass through upstream date format unmodified" decision (§6, T-022's original entry) while fixing the actual comparison bug — Qodo's own suggested fix allowed either normalized or original output; original was chosen since nothing about the contract or Cockpit's runtime validator requires a specific date format, and reformatting would be a needless deviation from the already-decided passthrough behavior.
2026-08-29 · [O3, T-030] · `quarantine` tags via Mailpit's `PUT /api/v1/tags` (`Tags: ["Quarantined"]`), never `DELETE /api/v1/messages` — the implementation-time question the row above deliberately left open. Two reasons, not one: (1) Mailpit's delete endpoint destroys the message outright, and worse, deletes **the entire mailbox** if `IDs` is empty or omitted (confirmed in the vendored `range/mailpit-api.json` spec) — a footgun with no equivalent on the tag endpoint. (2) A real security response contains a message for investigation, it doesn't destroy the only record of the attack — tagging is the behavior that actually matches what "quarantine" means outside this codebase. §17's demo beat (2:20-2:40, "Quarantined") only needs a visible state change, which tagging provides. The tool refuses an empty/all-blank `message_ids` at the server.py boundary regardless of which Mailpit endpoint is used, closing off the empty-IDs footgun categorically rather than relying on never calling delete being enough on its own
2026-08-30 · [O4, T-042] · **Eval harness measures "did the agent propose a gated action," not a three-way `VerdictLabel` — deliberately named `gate_trigger_accuracy`, never "agent verdict accuracy."** Investigated first, not assumed: `VerdictLabel` (`malicious|suspicious|legitimate`, `contracts/events.ts`) has no live producer anywhere in this project — T-037's own translator (`harness/translate/translate.ts`, PR #73, unmerged) explicitly declines to ever emit `mission.verdict`, on the record: "the stream carries only the model's prose; parsing a label out of prose is inventing semantics." Building a second, harness-side prose classifier would repeat exactly the mistake T-037's own author just declined to make, layering an invented classifier's error rate on top of the agent's. Instead this harness reads TrueForge's real `tool.approval_required` wire event directly (confirmed live T-002/§6, re-verified field-by-field against a running server's `openapi.json` in T-037, 2026-08-29) — that event can only ever fire for a tool named in `agent.json`'s `require_approval_for_tools`, so its bare occurrence is proof the agent proposed one of the four gated actions, with no classification invented on top. **This is a real, narrower claim than "verdict accuracy":** it cannot distinguish a correctly-judged-but-inconclusive "suspicious" case from a genuine miss, since the three-way label doesn't exist to test against — reported honestly as `gate_trigger_accuracy`/`false_positive_rate`, defined explicitly in `mission/eval/eval_lib.py`'s own docstring and `README.md`, not left implicit. Read the raw wire stream directly rather than depending on T-037's translator (still 3 PRs deep, unmerged, and scoped to Cockpit rendering, not evaluation) — `contracts/`/`harness/` untouched by this decision.
```

---

2026-08-30 · [O1, T-037] · **An unavailable IDENTITY finding is represented by ABSENCE — emit no `mission.evidence` event for that lane at all.** No producer exists for `lookalike_domain`/`lookalike_of` anywhere in `tools/imports_mcp`, and emitting `false` makes both cockpit render sites print "No lookalike domain detected" — a clean security finding nothing computed. Widening to `boolean | null` does **not** help: `null` is falsy, so both ternaries take the identical branch and print the same false negative, while `isBool` rejects it at runtime. Absence needs no contract change and no cockpit change — an empty lane is already handled and tested (cockpit `f9a19bd`, T-052), rendering "Waiting…"/"Nothing reported". `from_address`/`display_name`/`reply_to` are not lost, they already arrive on `mission.message_received`

2026-08-30 · [O1, T-037] · **A failed turn gets its own `mission.failed` event, as a discriminated union keyed on `cause`, not one shared `message` field.** Verified against a running server's openapi.json: `TurnStateError` has a **required** `message`, `TurnStateCancelled` has **no message at all** — only a required four-value `reason` enum. One shared string would have meant synthesising text and flattening that enum, the exact drift class T-037 exists to fix. Scope is terminal turn failure only — `TurnDoneEvent.state` has three members, one is success, hence exactly two causes. Subagent `ThreadStateError` and model `refusal`/`content_filter` are deliberately NOT force-fitted: both occur inside a turn that still reaches `turn.done`, so neither leaves the mission unterminated

2026-08-30 · [O2, T-032] · **A block rule is recorded, not enforced: a write-only local SQLite store owned by `imports-mcp`, and nothing reads it back.** §5 had this blocked since 2026-08-29 on "where does a block rule live and what enforces it", and the honest answer is *nothing does* — Mailpit is a mail catcher with no rule/filter/policy endpoint (checked in `range/mailpit-api.json`), the fake portal reads no configuration, and no component anywhere consumes a blocklist. The alternative was inventing an enforcement path to make the name true, which would have meant fabricating a demo beat. Chosen instead: make the *gate* real — a real tool call, a real human decision, a real durable record — and say plainly in the module docstring that no mail is blocked. §17 films the licence decision, not a blocked message, so the demo loses nothing. The row shape is chosen to be readable by a future enforcer rather than to foreclose one.

2026-08-30 · [O1, T-038] · **`contracts/events.ts` wins the `return_path` drift: the field stays on the message, and leaves IDENTITY's reported evidence list.** Two defensible readings existed in this file (§4's T-024 Qodo entry vs §7's entry the same day), so it needed a decision, not a guess. The contract is the wire format three components already validate against (`missionSource.ts`, `translate.ts`, the cockpit) while `agent.json`'s field list is prose instructing a model — changing the prose costs 2 lines, changing the contract costs a 2-approval `contracts/` PR plus every consumer, for a field `IdentityEvidence` never carried. IDENTITY still *compares* Return-Path (that instruction is untouched); it just stops claiming to report it.
